**Decision:** Wrap all three in `reliability.SafeGo`. `handlePipelineRun`'s goroutine gets a `PanicHandler` that runs `DefaultPanicHandler` for logging, then — under the existing `pipelineRunsMu` lock — sets `rec.Status = "failed"` and `rec.Error = fmt.Sprintf("panic: %v", panicVal)`, so a client polling `/api/pipelines/status` sees a definitive failure instead of an indefinite `"running"`. The `kg.BuildIndex()` startup goroutine uses `SafeGo` with a `nil` handler (log-and-continue; a failed KG build already degrades gracefully via its existing error-log branch). The DLQ replay-scan ticker loop is pulled out of its inline closure into a standalone `runDLQReplayScanOnce(dlq)` function — needed because `main()` can't run inside a unit test — which wraps its full per-tick body (`dlq.Reload()` plus each `dlq.Replay(id)`) in a synchronous `reliability.Recover`, and the ticker loop itself is wrapped in `SafeGo`; this mirrors the two-layer pattern ADR-048 established for `internal/llm/health.go`'s ticker (loop-level `SafeGo` plus a per-tick `Recover`), so a single poison-pill DLQ entry whose replay executor panics can't silently end every future scan tick.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-12) — closes ADR-048 milestones 4–5/5; the program's original 5-milestone scope is now complete.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A panicking pipeline step now resolves its run to `"failed"` with a non-empty `error` field instead of crashing `bt-dashboard` or leaving the run stuck at `"running"`. Pinned by `TestHandlePipelineRun_PanicRecoveredAsFailedStatus` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/pipeline_handlers_test.go`), a re-exec subprocess test since an unrecovered panic in the original bare goroutine would otherwise take the whole test binary down with it.
- ✅ The DLQ replay-scan ticker survives a panicking `dlq.Replay` call on one entry and still replays a later entry on the following tick, instead of the panic silently ending all future scans. Pinned by `TestDLQReplayScanSurvivesPanicAcrossTicks` (`cmd/bt-agent/main_test.go`), which injects a replay executor that panics on one entry and asserts a second, healthy entry is still replayed on the next simulated tick.
- ✅ Closes the "Q3 Reliability... extend SafeGo" program end to end: across ADR-046 → 047 → 048 → 049, every production `go func()` fan-out or long-running goroutine identified by the four passes is now wrapped, except the two sites noted below.
- ⚠️ `internal/agent/deploy_drift.go`'s own `StartDriftWatcher` ticker goroutine and `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`'s sprint-dispatch goroutine remain bare `go func()` calls — both already carry a hand-rolled `defer recover()` (so a panic there is caught today, just not through the standardized `SafeGo`/`PanicHandler` path), and neither was in scope for this program's four-pass sweep.
- Pinned by the tests named above.

---

## ADR-050: Wiring RebuildBackoff and Scheduler.AnyInFlight into the Production Deploy-Drift Watchers

**Context (2026-07-12):** Follow-up to ADR-045, whose own consequences flagged the gap: `RebuildBackoff` (retry-storm throttling) and `Scheduler.AnyInFlight()` (in-flight job protection) were implemented and unit-tested but dead code from the running daemon's perspective — `cmd/bt-agent/main.go`, `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`, and `cmd/bt-gardener/main.go` all constructed their `DriftWatchConfig` without a `Backoff`, and nothing consulted an in-flight check before a rebuild. A broken HEAD could therefore still retry-storm `go build` every 20-minute watcher tick in production, and a rebuild could still swap the live binary out from under an in-flight scheduled job.

**Decision:** `DriftWatchConfig` gains an `InFlightFn func() bool` field, consulted by `DriftWatchOnce` immediately before a rebuild attempt (right after the existing backoff-block check) — a `true` result skips the rebuild for that tick with the same `slog.Warn` pattern the backoff path uses. A new `agent.NewRebuildBackoff()` constructor returns the production-default guard (`MaxAttempts: 5`, `BaseDelay: 1 minute`, `MaxDelay: 30 minutes` — sized to stay inside one `DefaultDriftCheckInterval` watch cadence) and is now passed as `Backoff` in all three mains' `DriftWatchConfig` (`bt-agent`, `bt-dashboard`, `bt-gardener`). `bt-agent`'s watcher additionally wires `InFlightFn: globalSched.AnyInFlight`, since it is the only one of the three mains that owns a live `Scheduler`; `bt-dashboard` and `bt-gardener` leave `InFlightFn` unset (`nil` disables the guard, matching their lack of an equivalent in-process scheduler).

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-12) — closes the gap ADR-045's consequences flagged as "the natural next milestone."

**Consequences:**
- ✅ All three daemons now throttle rebuild attempts against a broken HEAD via `RebuildBackoff` instead of retrying every 20-minute tick indefinitely. Pinned by `TestDaemonDriftWatcherWiresBackoffAndInFlightGuard` and `TestGardenerDriftWatcherWiresRebuildBackoff` (`cmd/bt-agent/main_test.go`) and `TestDashboardDriftWatcherWiresRebuildBackoff` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`) — each a source-level audit for the wiring, since `main()` can't run inside a unit test.
- ✅ `bt-agent`'s watcher now skips a rebuild while any scheduled job is mid-execution. `DriftWatchOnce`'s new `InFlightFn` check is exercised end-to-end (not just source-audited) by `TestDriftWatchOnce_SkipsRebuildWhileJobInFlight` (`internal/agent/deploy_drift_test.go`), which also confirms a `nil` `InFlightFn` — `bt-dashboard`'s and `bt-gardener`'s case — leaves existing rebuild behavior unchanged.
- ⚠️ `bt-dashboard` and `bt-gardener` still have no in-flight guard of their own — neither owns a `Scheduler` instance, so there is nothing to plug into `InFlightFn` today; a rebuild triggered by either could still swap its own binary mid-request (`bt-dashboard`) or mid-cycle (`bt-gardener`). Only `bt-agent`'s scheduled-job case is covered.
- Pinned by the tests named above.

## ADR-051: Extending the Self-Healing Envelope to NSGA-II, the Last Zero-Observability Evolve Variant

**Context (2026-07-13):** Q2 Evolvability / Q3 Reliability program "Close the NSGA-II self-healing wiring gap, the last production Evolve variant with zero specialist/crisis observability." ADR-038 brought `Population.Evolve`, `Population.EvolveWithExperience`, `ParetoPopulation.EvolvePareto`, and `IslandModel.EvolveAll` onto the shared `selfHealGeneration` envelope, but `NSGAIIPopulation.Evolve` (`internal/evolution/multi_objective.go`) — the algorithm `bt_evolve_multiobjective` actually drives — was never migrated: `NewNSGAIIPopulation` left `Specialists` nil, and `Evolve` ran its own hand-rolled tournament-select/SBX-crossover/polynomial-mutation generation loop that called `nsga2.Evaluate` directly and never touched `Population.Crisis` or `Population.Specialists`. It was the one remaining `bt_evolve_*` GA/multi-objective tool with no crisis detection, no specialist archiving, and no resurrection path.

**Decision:** Mirror the pattern ADR-038 established for `EvolvePareto`. `NewNSGAIIPopulation` now seeds `pop.Specialists = NewSpecialistRegistry()` up front, the same nil-guard `IslandModel.EvolveAll` applies for islands added via the bare `AddIsland` path (`internal/evolution/island.go`). `NSGAIIPopulation.Evolve`'s per-generation body is rewritten to run entirely inside `nsga2.selfHealGeneration(eliteCount, supervisor, reproduce)`, where `reproduce` captures the existing tournament-selection/SBX-crossover/polynomial-mutation offspring step, environmental-selection combine-and-truncate by front rank and crowding distance, and re-`Evaluate` — because `selfHealGeneration` re-sorts `Individuals` by scalar `Fitness` before invoking `reproduce`, the closure re-runs `nsga2.Evaluate(fitnessFn)` first so `FitnessVecs`/`Fronts`/`CrowdingDist` stay in sync with the new order before tournament selection reads it. On the MCP side, `bt_evolve_multiobjective`'s handler (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) now calls `nsga.Specialists = evolution.SeedSpecialistRegistry()` right after constructing the population — overwriting `NewNSGAIIPopulation`'s empty default with the same benchmark-validated registry `newProductionPopulation` seeds for every other evolve tool — and the JSON response gains a `"health"` key from `evolveHealthProjection(nsga.Population)`, matching the `bt_evolve_genetic`/`bt_evolve_bottlenecks`/`bt_evolve_selection_pressure`/`bt_evolve_qd` response shape (ADR-037). Pinned by `TestNewNSGAIIPopulation_SeedsSpecialists` and `TestNSGAIIPopulation_Evolve_ResurrectsExtinctSpecialist` (`internal/evolution/multi_objective_test.go`, the latter driving a collapsing-diversity 10-individual population with a validated-but-absent goap archetype through one generation and asserting `diversity_collapse` is recorded, a `resurrected:true` individual is injected, and `Resurrections > 0`), and, on the MCP side, `TestBTEvolveMultiObjectiveRegisteredAndReturnsParetoMetrics`'s extended assertions on the `health` object's `crisis_reasons`/`resurrections`/`last_mutation_rate` fields and `TestBTEvolveMultiObjectiveSeedsSpecialistRegistry` (a source-level count of `evolution.SeedSpecialistRegistry()` call sites in `tools.go`, since the handler builds its population inside a `RegisterTool` closure with no exported hook to inspect after the fact).

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `bt_evolve_multiobjective` now runs the full ADR-031 intervention — crisis detection, emergency mutation rate, specialist-elite archiving, and resurrection — closing the last gap in the family of variants ADR-038 set out to unify
- ✅ Every `bt_evolve_*` GA/multi-objective/island tool except `bt_evolve_qlearning` and `bt_evolve_memetic` now shares one `selfHealGeneration` implementation instead of NSGA-II carrying an independent, self-healing-free copy of the generation loop
- ✅ `bt_evolve_multiobjective`'s response now surfaces `Population.HealthSnapshot()` under `health`, matching the sibling evolve tools, so an operator can observe crisis reasons/resurrections/applied mutation rate without re-running the tool with different logging
- ⚠️ `EvolveQLearning` and `MemeticEvolve` (ADR-038's residual gap) remain outside the envelope, as does `EvolveMAPElites`'s independent duplicate implementation — this program did not touch either

## ADR-052: Change-Impact Graph Exposed via a CLI Subcommand and an MCP Tool

**Context (2026-07-13):** NotebookLM research goal. `internal/knowledge/impact.go`'s `BuildImpactGraph`/`ImpactGraph.TestsFor` — import-edge and directory-proximity analysis mapping a changed source file to the tests it can affect — was complete and tested but had zero production consumers (§5.1's Responsibility Table called this out explicitly): nothing in `cmd/bt-agent`, `cmd/bt-agent-cli`, or the pre-commit gate (`make check-quick`) ever called it, so every commit still ran the full test suite regardless of how narrow the change was.

**Decision:** Add one shared entry point and two thin callers. `knowledge.ImpactedTests(root, source)` (`impact.go`) builds the graph for the module rooted at `root` and returns `TestsFor(source)` in a single call — the seam both new consumers share. `cmd/bt-agent-cli` gains an `impact <source-file> [--root <dir>]` subcommand (`cmd/bt-agent-cli/impact.go`): `normalizeImpactSource` converts a caller-supplied path (typically absolute, or relative to cwd) into the module-relative, slash-separated form the graph indexes, rejecting any path that resolves outside `root`, and `cmdImpact` prints one impacted test per line (or a "no impacted tests" message) for shell/pre-commit-hook consumption. `cmd/bt-agent` gains a matching `bt_impact_tests` MCP tool (`cmd/bt-agent/impact_tools.go`, registered from `registerMCPTools` via the new `registerImpactTools`), taking `source` (required, already module-relative) and an optional `root` (defaulting to the server process's working directory) and returning `{"tests": [...], "source": ...}` or `{"error": ...}`. Neither caller is yet wired into `make check-quick` or the pre-commit hook itself — both remain manually invocable surfaces for now. Pinned by `TestImpactedTests_BuildsGraphAndQueries`/`TestImpactedTests_UnknownSourceHasNoTests`/`TestImpactedTests_PropagatesBuildError` (`internal/knowledge/impact_test.go`), `TestNormalizeImpactSource_AbsoluteUnderRoot`/`TestNormalizeImpactSource_RejectsOutsideRoot`/`TestImpactedTestsForSource_AbsoluteSourceUnderRoot` (`cmd/bt-agent-cli/impact_test.go`), and `TestImpactTests_ReturnsImpactedTestsForSource`/`TestImpactTests_RejectsMissingSource`/`TestBTImpactTestsRegistered` (`cmd/bt-agent/impact_tools_test.go`).

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ The impact graph has its first production consumers — a CLI subcommand and an MCP tool — closing the "self-contained, no production consumer" gap §5.1 previously documented
- ✅ Both consumers share one build+query seam (`knowledge.ImpactedTests`), so a future change to graph-construction or query semantics lands once rather than needing synchronized edits in the CLI and MCP handlers
- ⚠️ Neither the CLI subcommand nor the MCP tool is wired into `make check-quick` or the git pre-commit hook yet, so no commit actually gates on a change-scoped test list today — both are opt-in tools an operator or a future automation must choose to invoke
- ⚠️ `BuildImpactGraph` rebuilds the whole module's import graph on every call — acceptable for an interactive CLI invocation or an occasional MCP call, but a commit-gate integration invoking it on every commit would want the graph cached or built incrementally rather than from scratch each time
- ⚠️ `normalizeImpactSource` (the abs-path/module-relative/outside-root-rejection logic) was private to `cmd/bt-agent-cli/impact.go`, so `bt_impact_tests` had no seam to reuse it and instead documented `source` as "already module-relative" — an MCP caller passing an absolute path got a silent empty test list rather than a normalized lookup or a rejection. ADR-070 (milestone 1/3) promotes it to `knowledge.NormalizeImpactSource`; **resolved by ADR-071 (2026-07-13):** the MCP tool now calls it too, closing the Q1 Correctness program.

---

## ADR-053: SafeGo/Recover Extended to the LLM Subprocess Scanner, the Fallback-Chain Call Loop, and the Scheduler/A2A Start Goroutines

**Context (2026-07-13):** Program "Q3 Reliability — harden the LLM subprocess and fallback-chain execution path against unrecovered panics and subprocess failure storms." The ADR-046→049 sweep wrapped every fan-out and long-running goroutine its four passes identified, but none of those passes touched the LLM call path itself, and two more bare `go` spawns in `cmd/bt-agent/main.go` went unnoticed by all four. `ACPClient.GenerateCtx`'s `scanJSONLines` goroutine (`internal/llm/acp.go:94`) parses newline-delimited JSON from an external ACP subprocess's stdout — attacker- or bug-controlled output a panic in `encoding/json` or the message-dispatch logic could turn into a crashed daemon. `FallbackLLM.generate` (`internal/llm/fallback.go`) calls each fallback model's `Generate`/`GenerateCtx`/`GenerateWithTimeout` directly in its retry loop; a panic from one model (a nil client, a malformed response triggering a bad type assertion) unwound past the loop instead of being recorded as that model's failure, defeating the whole point of a fallback chain. And `cmd/bt-agent/main.go` still ran `globalSched.Start(...)`'s callback registration (line ~362) and `a2aSrv.Start()` (line ~610) as bare `go` statements — a panic in either would take the scheduler or the A2A server down along with every other in-process subsystem.

**Decision:** Three milestones, one program. (1) `internal/llm/acp.go` gains a `startScanJSONLines(r, out, errc)` helper that launches `scanJSONLines` via `reliability.SafeGo("acp-scan-json-lines", ..., handler)`, where `handler` reports the panic on the same `errc` (`scanErr`) channel the scanner already uses for I/O errors — `GenerateCtx`'s existing `case err := <-scanErr` path handles a panic exactly like any other scan failure, no new error path needed. (2) `FallbackLLM.generate` wraps its `call(model.LLM)` invocation in a synchronous `reliability.Recover(model.Name, ...)`; a non-nil result is appended to `errs` as a wrapped error and the loop `continue`s to the next model, preserving the function's existing "collect errors, try the next model" contract. (3) `cmd/bt-agent/main.go` wraps both remaining bare spawns in `reliability.SafeGo` — `"scheduler-start"` around the `globalSched.Start` callback registration, `"a2a-server-start"` around the `a2aSrv.Start()` call — both with a `nil` handler (default log-and-continue), matching the `"kg-build-index"`/`"dlq-replay-scan-ticker"` pattern ADR-049 already established in the same file.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A malformed or adversarial ACP subprocess can no longer crash the daemon by panicking the stdout scanner; the panic surfaces as a normal `scanErr` failure for that one `GenerateCtx` call. Pinned by `TestStartScanJSONLines_ReaderPanicRecoveredOnScanErr` (`internal/llm/acp_test.go`).
- ✅ A panicking fallback model no longer aborts the whole `FallbackLLM.generate` chain — the panic becomes that model's error and the loop proceeds to the next model, exactly as a normal `error` return would. Pinned by `TestFallbackLLM_GenerateRecoversPanicAndTriesNextModel` (all-but-last model panics, chain still succeeds) and `TestFallbackLLM_GenerateAllPanicReturnsAggregatedError` (every model panics, the aggregated error is still returned rather than the process crashing) in `internal/llm/fallback_test.go`.
- ✅ `cmd/bt-agent/main.go`'s two remaining unguarded daemon-level goroutines — scheduler-start and A2A-server-start — are now covered, closing the gap the ADR-046→049 four-pass sweep left unaddressed. Pinned by `TestDaemonWrapsSchedulerAndA2AStartWithSafeGo`, a source-level assertion (`cmd/bt-agent/main_test.go`) since `main()` cannot run inside a unit test.
- ⚠️ This program's scope was the four sites named above; it is not a claim that every goroutine in `internal/llm/` is now guarded (e.g. `internal/llm/health.go`'s probes were already covered by ADR-048, but any LLM-adjacent goroutine outside these four sites was not audited as part of this pass).
- Pinned by the tests named above.

---

## ADR-054: Circuit Breaker on ACPClient Stops Failure-Storm Respawns of a Crash-Looping ACP Subprocess

**Context (2026-07-13):** Milestone 4/4 of the same "Q3 Reliability — harden the LLM subprocess and fallback-chain execution path against unrecovered panics and subprocess failure storms" program ADR-053 covered milestones 1–3 of. ADR-053 made a panicking scanner or fallback model degrade gracefully, but said nothing about a subprocess that simply fails to start or respond cleanly on every attempt — a missing `hermes` binary, a crash-looping agent process. Before this change, `ACPClient` had no memory of past failures: every `GenerateCtx` call unconditionally launched a fresh subprocess via `cmd.Start()`, so a persistently broken ACP command was relaunched (and timed out or errored) on every single call, burning a process spawn and a full timeout window per attempt with no backoff.

**Decision:** `ACPClient` gains a `breaker *reliability.CircuitBreaker` field, constructed once in `NewACPClient` as `reliability.NewCircuitBreaker("acp-subprocess:"+cfg.Command, 3, 60*time.Second)` — 3 consecutive failures trip the circuit for a 60s cooldown before a single half-open probe is allowed through. `GenerateCtx` checks `breaker.Allow()` immediately after its existing empty-command guard and before any subprocess work, returning a fast error naming the open circuit if the breaker rejects the call. Every failure exit on the subprocess-start/session-setup path — `StdinPipe`, `StdoutPipe`, `cmd.Start`, the ACP `initialize` request, `session/new`, a missing/blank `sessionId`, and the prompt-turn `request` call itself — now calls `breaker.RecordFailure()`; the sole success path (a non-error prompt response) calls `breaker.RecordSuccess()`. Because `internal/llm/provider.go`'s `NewProvider` constructs one long-lived `ACPClient` per daemon process (not per call), the breaker's failure count and open/half-open state persist for the life of the process instead of resetting between calls.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes the program ADR-053 began; all 4 milestones now complete.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A crash-looping or missing ACP subprocess is launched at most `threshold` (3) times before the breaker opens; every subsequent call within the 60s cooldown fails fast with a circuit-open error instead of paying a full subprocess-spawn-and-timeout cost. Pinned by `TestACPClientCircuitBreakerShortCircuitsRepeatedSubprocessFailures` (`internal/llm/acp_test.go`), which drives 10 `Generate` calls against a helper subprocess that always exits immediately and asserts the actual process-launch count stays below the call count, and that the final error mentions "circuit".
- ✅ A single transient failure does not trip the breaker — the 3-failure threshold and the existing per-call context timeout are unchanged, so an occasional slow or flaky subprocess start still gets retried on the next call as before.
- ⚠️ The breaker is keyed by `cfg.Command` on one `ACPClient` instance; if a caller ever constructs more than one `ACPClient` for the same command (outside the single-instance-per-daemon pattern `NewProvider` uses today), each instance gets its own independent breaker with no shared failure memory between them.
- Pinned by the test named above.

---

## ADR-055: RunTask Backstops bb.Result on Every Non-Success Terminal Outcome

**Context (2026-07-13):** NotebookLM research goal. `internal/agent/runner.go`'s `OutcomeErrorDetail` already substitutes a `"no run output"` sentinel for an empty `result.Output` so DLQ records and retry-exhaustion errors are never a bare `"agent outcome: failure"` — but that fallback lives one layer above the engine, in the one caller (`RunOnce`) that happens to check for an empty string. Any leaf that terminates a tree without success and without ever writing to `bb.Result` — a PreGate condition failing before `ExecutePlan`, a tree hitting the 1000-tick safety limit and falling into `RunTask`'s default (Partial) branch — left `bb.Result` (and `RunTask`'s own return value) blank for every other consumer: DLQ entries pushed directly from the engine, dashboards reading `bb.Result` off a `Blackboard`, and any future caller that doesn't happen to route through `internal/agent`. `TestRunTask_EmptyInput_Fails` and `TestGoDevTree_NonGoTask_Rejected` (`internal/engine/engine_test.go`) previously asserted the blank result directly, documenting the gap as expected behavior rather than a bug.

**Decision:** `RunTask` (`internal/engine/tree.go`), immediately after the terminal `switch` that sets `bb.Outcome` and before the existing tracing/`validateOutputQuality` calls, adds one backstop: `if bb.Outcome != string(evolution.Success) && bb.Result == "" { bb.Result = fmt.Sprintf("task %q produced no output (terminal outcome: %s, code: %d)", bb.Task, bb.Outcome, code) }`. This is additive only — a tree that already wrote a `bb.Result` (success or not) is untouched — and fires for every `RunTask` caller uniformly, not just the ones that separately check for an empty string downstream. `TestRunTask_EmptyInput_Fails` and `TestGoDevTree_NonGoTask_Rejected` were updated to assert a non-empty, outcome-naming result instead of an empty one.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ No `RunTask` caller can see a blank `bb.Result`/return value on a non-success outcome any more, regardless of which node type terminated the tree or which downstream consumer reads it — closing the "no run output"/"(last: unknown)" black hole at its source instead of patching each consumer individually. Pinned by `TestRunTask_BackstopsEmptyResultOnNonSuccess` (`internal/engine/tree_test.go`), which drives both an immediate-failure leaf and a perpetually-"running" leaf (forcing the 1000-tick/Partial path) through `RunTask` and asserts the backstop message names both the task and the terminal outcome.
- ✅ `internal/agent.OutcomeErrorDetail`'s own `"no run output"` fallback is now effectively unreachable from any `RunTask`-produced output — it still exists as a defensive fallback for callers that construct a `result.Output` outside the engine, but every engine-run task now arrives with a diagnosable message already attached.
- ⚠️ The backstop message is generated, not narrated by the failing node — it names the task and terminal outcome/code but cannot say *why* a specific condition failed if the node itself never recorded that detail; richer per-node failure narration remains a separate, unaddressed gap.
- Pinned by the test named above.

---

## ADR-056: HITL Escalated Requests Terminate Waits and Become Resolvable

**Context (2026-07-13):** NotebookLM research goal. `Store.WaitForRequest` (`internal/hitl/store_extensions.go`) polled a request's status and returned only on `StatusApproved`/`StatusSkipped` (success) or `StatusRejected`/`StatusExpired` (error); `StatusEscalated` — set by `Store.Escalate` when an operator escalates a request via the dashboard's `POST /api/hitl/<id>/escalate` handler (`internal/dashboard/hitl_handlers.go`) — matched neither case, so a waiter kept polling until its own context deadline instead of noticing the escalation. Worse, `Store.Approve`/`Store.Reject` (`internal/hitl/store.go`) both hard-required `r.Status == StatusPending`, so once a request was escalated, the two REST endpoints an operator would actually use to resolve it (`POST /api/hitl/<id>/approve`/`reject`) always failed with a "not pending" error — an escalated request could never be approved or rejected, only left to expire.

**Decision:** `WaitForRequest`'s switch adds `StatusEscalated` to the success case alongside `StatusApproved`/`StatusSkipped`, returning the request immediately instead of continuing to poll. `Store.Approve`/`Store.Reject` both relax their precondition from `r.Status != StatusPending` to `r.Status != StatusPending && r.Status != StatusEscalated`, so either can now resolve a request from either state.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `POST /api/hitl/<id>/approve` and `/reject` now succeed on an escalated request, closing a previously-unresolvable state — an operator can act on an escalation from the dashboard HITL panel instead of watching it sit until `StatusExpired`. Pinned by `TestApprove_FromEscalated`/`TestReject_FromEscalated` (`internal/hitl/store_extensions_test.go`).
- ✅ A waiter blocked in `WaitForRequest` (the production caller is `WorkflowApprovalWait`, `internal/dashboard/approval.go`) now returns as soon as a request is escalated instead of polling until its own context deadline. Pinned by `TestWaitForRequest_Escalate` (`internal/hitl/wait_test.go`).
- ⚠️ `WorkflowApprovalWait` treats any non-error `WaitForRequest` return as `Approved = true` without inspecting the returned request's `Status` — so a workflow step now proceeds as approved the moment its request is *escalated*, not once it is actually approved by the follow-up `Approve` call this same change enables. Escalation and approval are conflated on that call path; only the dashboard REST endpoints and the engine's per-tick `HumanApprovalGate` (`internal/engine/hitl_gate.go`, whose own status switch still treats `StatusEscalated` as an unhandled `default` → failure) distinguish the two. **Resolved by ADR-061 (2026-07-13):** `WorkflowApprovalWait` now branches on `resolved.Status` and only the engine's `HumanApprovalGate` default-to-failure gap remains.
- Pinned by the tests named above.

---

## ADR-057: Deterministic `bt_evolve_pareto` MCP Entry Point for `ParetoPopulation.EvolvePareto`

**Context (2026-07-13):** NotebookLM research goal. Since ADR-038 milestone 4, `ParetoPopulation.EvolvePareto` (`internal/evolution/pareto.go`) already wrapped each generation in the shared `selfHealGeneration` self-healing envelope — the same crisis-detection/specialist-resurrection machinery `bt_evolve_qd`/`bt_evolve_island`/`bt_evolve_multiobjective` drive — but no `bt_evolve_*` MCP tool ever constructed a `ParetoPopulation`, so the algorithm and its self-healing were reachable only from tests, unlike its GA/multi-objective/island siblings.

**Decision:** Add `bt_evolve_pareto` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`), mirroring the existing deterministic evolve-tool pattern. Like `bt_evolve_qd`/`bt_evolve_island`/`bt_evolve_multiobjective`, it builds its population through `newProductionPopulation` (not `evolution.NewParetoPopulation`) so the population's `Specialists` registry is pre-seeded via `SeedSpecialistRegistry`, backing crisis resurrection on this path too; it then wraps that `*evolution.Population` and a `NewParetoFront` over the fixed five structural dimensions (`DimSuccessRate`/`DimPathCoverage`/`DimStability`/`DimNodeEfficiency`/`DimExecutionSpeed`) into a `ParetoPopulation` by hand. It calls `EvolvePareto(generations, StructuralMultiFitness)` and reports `ParetoFront.Stats()` verbatim — `front_size`, `diversity_score`, `best_per_dim` — alongside `node_count` and `health` (`evolveHealthProjection(pp.Population)`, the same `Population.HealthSnapshot()` projection every other evolve tool uses). An unresolvable `tree` id returns the shared `{"error":"unknown tree"}` shape rather than a partial or panicking result.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `EvolvePareto`'s front-elitism selection (`SelectPareto`-driven crossover across the Pareto front, distinct from `bt_evolve_multiobjective`'s full NSGA-II non-dominated sort + crowding distance over a separate `NSGAIIPopulation`) is now reachable in production, and its ADR-038 self-healing envelope gets a real caller for the first time — `health` is present from this tool's first release, unlike `bt_evolve_multiobjective`, which needed a dedicated follow-up (ADR-051) to gain crisis detection after shipping without it.
- ✅ bt-agent's registered-tool count grows by one (`registerMCPTools`'s own doc comment: 78 → 79).
- ⚠️ `bt_evolve_pareto` and `bt_evolve_multiobjective` now report two structurally different, non-interchangeable shapes for a conceptually similar "multi-objective front" result — `front_size`/`diversity_score`/`best_per_dim` vs `pareto_front_size`/`dimension_bests` — a caller switching between the two tools cannot treat their responses as the same schema.
- Pinned by `TestBTEvolveParetoRegisteredAndReturnsParetoMetrics` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`).

## ADR-058: `DecisionTrace.Steps` Populated from `bb.ChildTicks()` for `ExplainLastFailure`

**Context (2026-07-13):** NotebookLM research goal. `internal/agent/scheduler.go`'s two production `DecisionTrace` record sites (`RunNow`, `runJob`) always recorded a trace with `Steps` left at its zero value (nil), so `KnowledgeGraph.ExplainLastFailure` (`internal/knowledge/traces.go`, exposed as the `bt_kg_explain` MCP tool) rendered a `Path:` header with nothing under it for every run — the "explain why this tree last failed" feature had no execution path to show, regardless of how the tree actually failed.

**Decision:** `RunResult` (`internal/agent/runner.go`) gains a `ChildTicks []engine.ChildTick` field (`json:"-"`, so the JSON contract is unchanged), populated from `bb.ChildTicks()` alongside the existing `NodePaths = bb.VisitedPaths` assignment. `internal/knowledge` gains a local `ChildTick{Parent,Child,Status}` mirror (avoiding a new dependency on `internal/engine`) and `StepsFromChildTicks(ticks []ChildTick) []TraceStep`, which converts each tick into a `TraceStep{NodeName: tick.Child, ParentName: tick.Parent, Status: tick.Status}`, skipping ticks with an empty `Child`. Both scheduler trace sites build a small `stepsFromChildTicks` adapter converting `engine.ChildTick` into `knowledge.ChildTick`, then set `DecisionTrace.Steps` from it before calling `knowledge.GlobalTraceStore.Record`.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `ExplainLastFailure` now renders a genuine, non-empty execution path for every trace recorded through the two scheduler sites — pinned end-to-end by `TestExplainLastFailure_RendersPathFromChildTicks` (`internal/knowledge/traces_test.go`), alongside unit coverage for the conversion itself (`TestStepsFromChildTicks_*`).
- ⚠️ The bridge sets only `NodeName`/`ParentName`/`Status` on each `TraceStep` — `NodeType` is left blank, since `engine.ChildTick` carries no type information. `RecordSelectorOutcomes` (`internal/knowledge/traces.go`, the ADR-029 milestone-3 trace bridge), which selects steps by `NodeType == "Selector"`, therefore still finds nothing to record from these traces and remains uncalled outside tests; Selector-ordering telemetry keeps flowing through its own already-live route, `flushSelectorTelemetry` (`internal/agent/runner.go`), which reads the same `ChildTicks` independently and is unaffected by this change.
- Pinned by the tests named above.

---

## ADR-059: SafeGo Extended to the Webhook-Publisher Loop and the RateLimiter/SessionStore Cleanup Goroutines (milestones 1–3/5)

**Context (2026-07-13):** Program "Q3 Reliability — close the second-wave SafeGo panic-recovery gap in production-wired security and webhook goroutines." The ADR-046→049/053 sweeps covered A2A/dashboard/knowledge-graph fan-out, `reactive_parallel`, the LLM health monitor and request-timeout middleware, the dashboard pipeline-run and bt-agent KG-index/DLQ-scan goroutines, and the LLM subprocess/fallback-chain path — but three more production-wired bare `go` spawns remained. `WebhookPublisher.Attach` (`internal/agent/webhook_publisher.go`, wired at `cmd/bt-agent/main.go:593`) forwards every `AgentBus` event's arbitrary `Data interface{}` payload through `json.Marshal` and an HMAC-signed POST; a panic there — e.g. an unmarshalable `Data` value from any producer — would crash the whole `bt-agent` daemon. `NewRateLimiter` (`internal/security/security.go`, wired at `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go:251`) and `NewSessionStore` (`internal/security/session.go`, wired at `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go:214` and `:337`) each spawn a background cleanup goroutine that periodically ranges over a shared map under a mutex; a panic in either — a corrupted map entry, for instance — would crash the whole `bt-dashboard` process, distinct from `RequestTimeoutMiddleware`'s already-guarded panic path in the same file (ADR-048).

**Decision:** Wrap all three spawns in `reliability.SafeGo`. `WebhookPublisher.Attach` launches `p.loop` via `SafeGo("webhook-publisher-loop", p.loop, nil)`; because `loop()` is a single long-running `for`/`select`, an unrecovered panic inside it would otherwise silently stop forwarding all future events even once the process itself survives, so `loop()` additionally wraps each `p.handleEvent(event)` call in a synchronous `reliability.Recover("webhook-publisher-handle-event", ...)`, keeping the loop alive across a single panicking event. `NewRateLimiter` is refactored into a new exported `NewRateLimiterWithCleanupInterval(rate, burst, cleanupInterval)` (so tests can drive a fast cleanup cadence) that launches `rl.cleanup` via `SafeGo("rate-limiter-cleanup", rl.cleanup, nil)`; `NewRateLimiter` becomes a thin wrapper calling it with the existing 10-minute default. `NewSessionStore` launches `ss.cleanupLoop` via `SafeGo("session-store-cleanup", ss.cleanupLoop, nil)`. Unlike the webhook loop, `cleanup` and `cleanupLoop` are wrapped only at the outer-loop level — no per-tick `Recover` — matching the single-layer treatment `RequestTimeoutMiddleware` and most ADR-046/047 sites received, rather than the two-layer pattern ADR-048/049 established for `internal/llm/health.go`'s ticker and the DLQ replay-scan ticker.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — milestones 1–3 of 5.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A panic while forwarding a malformed `AgentEvent.Data` payload to Hermes no longer crashes `bt-agent`, and — because `handleEvent` is separately recovered — the publisher keeps forwarding subsequent events afterward instead of going silently idle. Pinned by `TestWebhookPublisherLoop_PanicRecovered` (`internal/agent/webhook_publisher_test.go`), a subprocess test mirroring `TestHealthMonitorStart_PanicRecovered`'s pattern, asserting normal events still reach the server despite interleaved panicking ones.
- ✅ A panic in `RateLimiter.cleanup` or `SessionStore.cleanupLoop` — e.g. a corrupted bucket or session entry — no longer crashes `bt-dashboard`. Pinned by `TestRateLimiterCleanup_PanicRecovered` (`internal/security/security_test.go`) and `TestSessionCleanup_PanicRecovered` (`internal/security/session_test.go`), both subprocess tests that seed a nil map entry to force a panic on the next tick and assert the process is still alive afterward.
- ⚠️ `RateLimiter.cleanup` and `SessionStore.cleanupLoop` only get the single-layer guard: `SafeGo` recovers the panic and the process survives, but the panicking goroutine itself still exits, so periodic bucket/session cleanup silently stops for the rest of that process's life after a single cleanup-tick panic — a memory-growth risk distinct from a crash. Neither test above asserts cleanup resumes after the panic, only that the process stays alive.
- ⚠️ This is milestones 1–3 of a 5-milestone program; three more bare `go` spawns remain, but all three are currently dormant with no production caller — `internal/security/apikey.go`'s `KeyRotationScheduler.loop`, `internal/config/watcher.go`'s `ConfigWatcher.loop`, and `internal/reliability/heartbeat.go`'s `NodeHeartbeat.cleanupLoop` (both constructors) — lower urgency than the three closed here, which were all reachable from a running daemon. **Milestones 4–5 closed the same day by ADR-060.**
- Pinned by the tests named above.

---

## ADR-060: SafeGo Extended to the Key-Rotation, Config-Watcher, and Node-Heartbeat Cleanup Goroutines (closes milestones 4–5/5)

**Context (2026-07-13):** Program "Q3 Reliability — close the second-wave SafeGo panic-recovery gap in production-wired security and webhook goroutines," milestones 4–5/5 — the three sites ADR-059 explicitly left open as "currently dormant with no production caller." `KeyRotationScheduler.Start` (`internal/security/apikey.go:301`) spawns `go krs.loop()`, whose `rotate()` invokes a caller-supplied `onRotate` callback — arbitrary application code with no guarantees — making it the highest panic-surface site in this set even without a `cmd/*/main.go` wiring today. `ConfigWatcher.Start` (`internal/config/watcher.go:120`) spawns `go w.loop()`, which runs registered `OnChange` callbacks synchronously in the watcher goroutine. `NewNodeHeartbeat` and `NewNodeHeartbeatWithCleanupInterval` (`internal/reliability/heartbeat.go:56`, `:69`) each independently spawn `go hb.cleanupLoop()`.

**Decision:** Wrap all three in `reliability.SafeGo`. `KeyRotationScheduler.Start` launches `krs.loop` via `SafeGo("key-rotation-scheduler", krs.loop, nil)`; because `onRotate` is caller-supplied and the highest-risk call in the loop, `rotate()` additionally wraps each `krs.onRotate(oldHash, newKey)` invocation in a synchronous `reliability.Recover("key-rotation-onRotate", ...)`, matching the two-layer loop-plus-per-call pattern ADR-059 established for `WebhookPublisher.loop`/`handleEvent` — a panicking callback can no longer end all future rotations. `ConfigWatcher.Start` launches `w.loop` via `SafeGo("config-watcher-loop", w.loop, nil)`. Both `NewNodeHeartbeat` and `NewNodeHeartbeatWithCleanupInterval` launch their respective `hb.cleanupLoop` via `SafeGo("node-heartbeat-cleanup-loop", hb.cleanupLoop, nil)` — single-layer, matching the `RateLimiter.cleanup`/`SessionStore.cleanupLoop` treatment from milestones 1–3, since there is no per-item callback to isolate.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes ADR-059 milestones 4–5/5; the program's 5-milestone scope is now complete.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A panic inside a caller-supplied `onRotate` callback no longer crashes the process the scheduler runs in, and subsequent scheduled rotations keep firing. Pinned by `TestKeyRotationScheduler_StartSurvivesOnRotatePanic` (`internal/security/apikey_test.go`), which forces a panic on every `onRotate` call and asserts a later `RotateNow` still rotates keys.
- ✅ A panicking `OnChange` callback in `ConfigWatcher` no longer crashes the watching process, and polling continues afterward. Pinned by `TestConfigWatcherLoop_PanicRecovered` (`internal/config/watcher_test.go`), a subprocess test.
- ✅ A panicking cleanup tick in either `NodeHeartbeat` constructor's goroutine no longer crashes the process. Pinned by `TestNodeHeartbeatCleanup_PanicRecovered` and `TestNodeHeartbeatWithCleanupIntervalCleanup_PanicRecovered` (`internal/reliability/heartbeat_test.go`), both subprocess tests covering the two independent spawn sites.
- ⚠️ Like `RateLimiter.cleanup`/`SessionStore.cleanupLoop` in milestones 1–3, `ConfigWatcher.loop` and `NodeHeartbeat.cleanupLoop` get only the single-layer guard: the process survives a panic, but the panicking goroutine itself exits, so config polling / node-heartbeat cleanup silently stops for the rest of that process's life after a single panic. `KeyRotationScheduler.loop` avoids this for the common case by isolating `onRotate` specifically, but a panic inside `loop()` itself (outside `rotate()`) still ends the loop for good.
- ⚠️ All three sites remain dormant with no `cmd/*/main.go` caller today — this closes the standardized-panic-recovery gap ADR-059 flagged, not a live production risk; the fix is defensive/forward-looking, guarding whichever daemon wires these in next.
- ✅ Closes the "Q3 Reliability — close the second-wave SafeGo panic-recovery gap" program end to end: across ADR-059 → ADR-060, all six sites the second-wave sweep identified are now wrapped.
- Pinned by the tests named above.

---

## ADR-061: `WorkflowApprovalWait` and `FindPendingByTaskID` Stop Treating an Escalated HITL Request as Approved (milestones 1–3/4)

**Context (2026-07-13):** Program "Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability — stop HITL escalation from silently auto-approving workflow steps." ADR-056 made `Store.WaitForRequest` return as soon as a request's status became `StatusEscalated`, alongside its two success statuses `StatusApproved`/`StatusSkipped` — but `WorkflowApprovalWait` (`internal/dashboard/approval.go`) discarded the returned `*hitl.Request` and set `result.Approved = true` unconditionally on any non-error return, so an escalated request was indistinguishable from a genuinely approved one on this call path. Downstream, `internal/dashboard/workflow_orchestrator.go`'s `executeStep` mapped that false `Approved = true` straight to `sr.Outcome = "success"` / `sr.Output = "approved"`, so an escalation let dependent side-effecting pipeline steps run as if a human had signed off. Separately, `internal/hitl/store_extensions.go`'s `FindPendingByTaskID` still filtered on `r.Status != StatusPending` only, so `ApproveByTaskID`/`RejectByTaskID` — the by-task-ID resolution path a dashboard operator or the REST handlers would use — could not find an escalated request at all, even though ADR-056 had already relaxed `Store.Approve`/`Store.Reject`'s own precondition to accept it.

**Decision:** `WorkflowApprovalWait` now captures the `*hitl.Request` returned by `WaitForRequest` as `resolved` and switches on `resolved.Status`: `Approved = true` only for `StatusApproved`/`StatusSkipped`; a new `Escalated bool` field on `ApprovalWaitResult` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_orchestrator.go`) is set for `StatusEscalated`, with `Approved` left `false`. `executeStep`'s `StepApproval` case checks `res.Escalated` before its existing error handling and gives it a distinct terminal shape — `sr.Outcome = "escalated"`, `sr.Output = "approval escalated"`, `sr.Error = "approval escalated for human review"` — instead of falling into the approved path. The `Run` loop's abort/compensation gate (previously `sr.Outcome == "failure" || sr.Outcome == "timeout" || sr.Outcome == "rejected"`) adds `|| sr.Outcome == "escalated"`, so an escalated approval now halts the pipeline (respecting `step.OnFailure`) the same way a rejection does. `FindPendingByTaskID`'s filter becomes `r.Status != StatusPending && r.Status != StatusEscalated`, so it returns an escalated request the same as a pending one and `ApproveByTaskID`/`RejectByTaskID` can resolve it.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — milestones 1–3/4; a fourth milestone in the same program remains open.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ An escalated HITL request can no longer be mistaken for an approval on the `WorkflowApprovalWait` path: `Approved` stays `false` and the step outcome is a distinct `"escalated"`, not `"success"`. Pinned by `TestWorkflowApprovalWait_EscalatedIsNotApproved` (`internal/dashboard/approval_test.go`), which drives a real `hitl.Store` (not the mocked `WaitApproval`) through `StatusEscalated`.
- ✅ A workflow whose approval step escalates now halts (per `step.OnFailure`) instead of letting later steps run on the strength of a non-approval. Pinned by `TestWorkflow_ApprovalEscalatedIsDistinctAndHalts` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_orchestrator_test.go`).
- ✅ `ApproveByTaskID`/`RejectByTaskID` can now resolve an escalated request looked up by task ID, matching the direct-by-ID `Store.Approve`/`Store.Reject` capability ADR-056 already added. Pinned by `TestApproveByTaskID_FromEscalated`/`TestRejectByTaskID_FromEscalated` (`internal/hitl/store_extensions_test.go`).
- ⚠️ This closes the specific gap ADR-056 flagged in its own consequences (see that entry, now marked resolved) but is scoped to milestones 1–3 of a 4-milestone program; the remaining milestone is not addressed here. **Resolved by ADR-068 (2026-07-13):** the by-task-ID REST/MCP resolution paths now also surface which state a resolved request came from, closing milestone 4/4 of the same program.
- Pinned by the tests named above.

---

## ADR-062: Cross-Domain Experience Transfer Closes the Island Model's Own Feedback Loop (Q2 Evolvability, milestone 4/4)

**Context (2026-07-13):** ADR-017/ADR-021 made mutation experience durable across restarts and shared it across the daemon and gardener binaries, but `IslandModel.Migrate` (`internal/evolution/island.go`) — which copies elite individuals from a fitter source domain island into a weaker target domain island every migration cycle — moved genomes without moving the experience that made them fit. The seam existed but was broken: `ExperienceBank.TransferExperiences(sourceTree, targetTree)` ignored `sourceTree` entirely and instead called `Retrieve(targetTree, 5)`, the Jaccard-similarity query — so the entries it returned were whatever was already similar to `targetTree`'s own name, with no actual provenance from the domain the migrating individuals came from. No caller invoked it from `Migrate` either, so the stub was doubly dead.

**Decision:** `TransferExperiences(sourceTree, _)` now calls `RetrieveByTreeType(sourceTree, 5)`, making it genuinely source-tree-aware. A new `ExperienceBank.SeedDomain(sourceTree, targetTree)` copies up to 5 of those entries into `targetTree`'s own pool via the existing `addEntry` path — each copy gets a fresh ID (`transfer_<src>_to_<tgt>_<origID>_<nanos>`) so it never collides with or dedupes against the original, is re-tagged `TreeType = targetTree`, and has `TimesReused`/`CreatedAt` reset to look like a native entry. `IslandModel` gains an optional `Bank *ExperienceBank` field (`json:"-"`, caller-wired, nil-safe); `Migrate` tracks `pairMigrated` per domain pair and, when `Bank != nil && pairMigrated > 0`, calls `Bank.SeedDomain(srcDomain, tgtDomain)` once per pair after the migration copy completes.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes milestone 4/4 of "Close the experience-grounded evolution feedback loop" (Q2 Evolvability).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Experience now compounds across sibling domain trees, not just across restarts (ADR-017/ADR-018) and across binaries (ADR-021/ADR-022) — a mutation strategy proven on one domain seeds the pool of every domain its elites migrate into
- ✅ The previous `TransferExperiences` stub is fixed to match its own doc comment's claim (source-tree-aware transfer) rather than silently querying the wrong tree
- ✅ **Closed 2026-07-13 (ADR-076):** `bt_evolve_island` now assigns `im.Bank = deps.expBank` immediately after `evolution.NewIslandModel(...)`, so the one production caller of `IslandModel.Migrate` exercises the transfer live instead of only from tests
- Pinned by tests in `internal/evolution/island_model_test.go` and, for the production wiring, `TestBTEvolveIslandWiresExperienceBankIntoMigration` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`)

---

## ADR-063: Durable Per-Agent Circuit Breaker State Feeds the Dashboard's cb_status Column

**Context (2026-07-13):** `internal/dashboard/agents.go`'s `loadCircuitBreakers`/`ListAgentsWithCB` were fully built and tested to read `agent.CircuitBreakersFile()` (`circuit_breakers.json`) into each agent's `cb_status` field — but `AgentCircuitBreakerStore` (`internal/agent/scheduler_cb.go`), the scheduler's actively-used per-agent breaker (consulted on every run via `Allowed`/`validateAgentRun` and updated via `reportAgentOutcome`), tracked all state in memory only. The file had zero writers anywhere in the repo, so `cb_status` rendered `"unknown"` for every agent regardless of actual breaker state, and a process restart silently forgot which agents had tripped.

**Decision:** Add `AgentCircuitBreakerStore.Save(path)`/`.Load(path)`. `Save` snapshots every tracked breaker's `state.String()`/`failureCount`/`lastFailureTime` (RFC3339, omitted when zero) into the exact `{"breakers": {name: {status, failures, last_failure}}}` shape `loadCircuitBreakers` already decodes, written via the standard tmp+rename idiom. `Load` reconstructs breakers from that shape via `NewAgentCircuitBreaker`, restoring status/failure count/last-failure time; a missing file is a silent no-op (first-boot case), matching the missing-file-no-error contract used elsewhere in the codebase (feedback persistence, DLQ, ExperienceBank). `Scheduler.runJob` calls `cbStore.Save(CircuitBreakersFile())` immediately after `reportAgentOutcome` on every run; `NewScheduler` calls `cbStore.Load(CircuitBreakersFile())` once at construction, before `ReconcileWithRegistry`.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ The dashboard's `cb_status` column now reflects real, persisted breaker state instead of always reading an absent file
- ✅ A restart no longer forgets that an agent's circuit was open — the breaker resumes in the state it was saved in rather than cold-starting closed
- ✅ (2026-07-13, ADR-065) The separate per-winner breakers ADR-064 adds inside the A2A auction path now also persist into the same `circuit_breakers.json` file, namespaced under an `"a2a.auction.winner."` key prefix so the two populations coexist in one file without a shared Go type — closing milestone 5 of the A2A reliability program. The two populations are still read by no single unified dashboard view; only the scheduler's own per-agent breakers feed `cb_status`
- Pinned by `TestAgentCircuitBreakerStore_Save_WritesDashboardShape`, `TestAgentCircuitBreakerStore_SaveThenLoad_RestoresState`, `TestAgentCircuitBreakerStore_Load_MissingFileIsNotError`, and the end-to-end `TestScheduler_PersistsCircuitBreakerOnFailure` (`internal/agent/scheduler_cb_test.go`)

---

## ADR-064: Honest A2A SendTask Errors and Per-Winner Retry/Circuit-Breaking in Auction Dispatch (A2A Reliability Program, milestones 1 & 3 of 5)

**Context (2026-07-13):** `BTAgentClient.SendTask` (`internal/a2a/client.go`) returned a nil error for every outcome that was not a genuine success — an empty `Message`, any non-completed `Task.Status.State` (failed/canceled/rejected, not only completed-with-no-artifact), and an unrecognized response type all formatted a description of the failure as the returned string and reported success. `AuctionDelegate`/`RunAuction` (`internal/a2a/auction.go`) therefore could not tell a failed dispatch from a real one — a losing outcome surfaced as `AuctionResult.Result` text describing its own failure rather than an error the caller could react to. Separately, `RunAuction` dispatched the auction winner exactly once: no retry for a transient failure, no circuit breaker to stop hammering a winner that fails on every auction, and no runner-up fallback.

**Decision:** Extract `SendTask`'s response-interpretation logic into `interpretSendResult(resp a2a.SendMessageResult) (string, error)`. It returns a non-nil `error` for every non-success case — `"a2a: agent message carried no text content"`, `"a2a: task %s did not complete: state=%s status=%s"` for any non-`TaskStateCompleted` state, `"a2a: task %s completed with no artifact text"`, and `"a2a: agent returned unrecognized response type %T"` — and only ever returns a nil error alongside genuine extracted text. On the dispatch side, `Auctioneer` gains a `breakers map[string]*reliability.CircuitBreaker` keyed by winner name (lazily created in `winnerBreaker`, threshold 3 failures, 30s cooldown) and consults `breaker.Allow()` before every dispatch, refusing immediately with an error if the circuit is open. When allowed, `RunAuction` wraps the `SendTask` call in a `reliability.RetryPolicy` (3 retries, 50ms–500ms full-jitter-free backoff) bounded by the same `candidateContext` deadline already used for the dispatch; every retry exhaustion calls `breaker.RecordFailureWithCategory`, every success calls `breaker.RecordSuccess`.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes milestones 1 (honest `SendTask` errors) and 3 (per-candidate breaker + retry) of the 5-milestone "A2A reliability adoption" program; milestones 2 and 4 (SafeGo across the A2A/dashboard fan-out and daemon background loops) were already closed by ADR-046/ADR-047 and the SafeGo second-wave passes. Milestone 5 (persisting these per-winner breakers into the shared `circuit_breakers.json` file ADR-063 now writes) closed the same day by ADR-065.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A failed winner dispatch is now a real `error` the caller (`engine/actions_a2a.go`'s `AuctionDelegate` action) can detect and react to, instead of success text describing a failure — closing a silent-failure class in the same family the platform already guards against elsewhere (§8.12, §8.13, §8.15)
- ✅ A transient dispatch failure is retried up to 3 times before the auction gives up on that winner, and a persistently-failing winner is circuit-broken after 3 failures rather than being redispatched to (and re-timed-out by) every subsequent auction
- ⚠️ `CollectBids`' existing behavior of treating `SendTask` errors identically to an empty/declined response is unaffected — bid fan-out semantics don't change, only the winner-dispatch path does
- ⚠️ No runner-up fallback yet: if the winner's breaker is open or all retries are exhausted, `RunAuction` still fails the auction outright rather than trying the next-best bid
- ✅ (2026-07-13, ADR-065) Per-winner breaker state now persists both across calls (the production `AuctionDelegate` entrypoint had been discarding its `Auctioneer` — and thus its breaker map — on every single engine tick, so the breaker guarding the real production path could never actually open) and across restarts, via a process-wide store round-tripped through `circuit_breakers.json`
- Pinned by tests in `internal/a2a/client_test.go`: `TestInterpretSendResult_HonestlyReportsFailureStates` (honest-error coverage per response shape) and `TestAuctioneer_RunAuction_RetriesWinnerDispatchOnTransientFailure`/`_ReturnsErrorAfterExhaustingRetries`/`_CircuitBreaksWinnerAfterRepeatedFailures` (retry/circuit-breaker behavior); milestone 5 pinned by `TestAuctionDelegate_WinnerCircuitBreakerSurvivesAcrossCallsAndRestarts` (ADR-065)

---

## ADR-065: A2A Auction Winner Circuit Breakers Persist Across Engine Ticks and Restarts (A2A Reliability Program, milestone 5 of 5)

**Context (2026-07-13):** ADR-064's per-winner `reliability.CircuitBreaker` only ever worked in theory in production. `AuctionDelegate` (`internal/a2a/auction.go`) — the sole seam `engine.AuctionDelegateFn` calls in production, on every engine tick that reaches an `AuctionDelegate` node — built a brand-new `Auctioneer` via `NewAuctioneer(newAuctionCollector())` on every single invocation. Because the breaker lives on `Auctioneer.breakers`, a fresh `Auctioneer` means a fresh, empty breaker map: a winner's failure count reset to zero on every tick, so the circuit guarding the real production path could never actually open no matter how many times that winner failed. `TestAuctioneer_RunAuction_CircuitBreaksWinnerAfterRepeatedFailures` (ADR-064) only proved the breaker works when one `Auctioneer` is reused across `RunAuction` calls — a shape production never exercises. Separately, even a reused `Auctioneer`'s breaker state was in-memory only and did not survive a process restart, unlike the scheduler's per-agent breakers ADR-063 made durable the same day.

**Decision:** Give winner breakers a process-wide, file-backed home instead of an `Auctioneer`-instance-scoped one. A new `winnerBreakerStore` (`internal/a2a/auction.go`) holds one `*reliability.CircuitBreaker` per winner name for the process lifetime, keyed with an `"a2a.auction.winner."` prefix; a single package-level instance, `winnerBreakers`, is shared by every caller. `NewPersistentAuctioneer(transport)` — the constructor `AuctionDelegate` now calls instead of `NewAuctioneer` — sets an `Auctioneer.persistent` flag that routes `winnerBreaker(name)` through `winnerBreakers.get(name)` instead of the per-instance map; `NewAuctioneer` is unchanged and keeps ad hoc/test callers on the cheap, disk-free per-instance map. `winnerBreakerStore.get` lazily loads persisted state from `agent.CircuitBreakersFile()` on first use (`loadLocked`, reading only its own `"a2a.auction.winner."`-prefixed keys so it never touches the scheduler's per-agent entries in the same file, and replaying the persisted failure count through `reliability.CircuitBreaker.RecordFailure` since the type exposes no direct state-setter). `RunAuction` calls the new `persistWinnerBreakerState()` after every dispatch outcome (success or failure), which merges the in-memory breaker states — via two new `reliability.CircuitBreaker` accessors, `FailureCount()` and `LastFailureTime()` — into whatever the file already holds and writes it back via tmp+rename, so the scheduler's own per-agent entries in the same file are preserved rather than clobbered. A save failure is logged, not returned, matching the scheduler's tolerant persist-after-outcome pattern — a disk hiccup must never fail the auction itself.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes milestone 5 of the 5-milestone "A2A reliability adoption" program (ADR-064); the program is now complete end to end.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A winner's failure count now survives across engine ticks, which is what production actually does — the circuit breaker can finally open in the deployed path, not just in a same-`Auctioneer` unit test
- ✅ A tripped winner breaker also survives a process restart, closing the gap ADR-064's Consequences flagged relative to ADR-063's scheduler-side durability
- ✅ Winner breakers and the scheduler's per-agent breakers now share one file (`circuit_breakers.json`) without a shared Go type or clobbering each other, via key-prefix namespacing and merge-on-save
- ⚠️ No unified dashboard view yet: `loadCircuitBreakers`/`ListAgentsWithCB` (`internal/dashboard/agents.go`) key strictly by agent name, so the `"a2a.auction.winner."`-prefixed entries are persisted but not surfaced in `cb_status` or anywhere else in the dashboard — reading them is a follow-up, not a code gap in this ADR
- Pinned by `TestAuctionDelegate_WinnerCircuitBreakerSurvivesAcrossCallsAndRestarts` (`internal/a2a/client_test.go`), which drives the real `AuctionDelegate` entrypoint across 8 simulated engine ticks against a permanently-failing winner, asserts the dispatch-call count plateaus once the breaker opens, and asserts the persisted `circuit_breakers.json` carries an `"open"` status for `"a2a.auction.winner.bad-winner"`

---

## ADR-066: Personal-Tree Reflection Evidence Keyed on the Real Tree ID and Owning User; `bankFor` Stops Permanently Caching a Transient Open Failure

**Context (2026-07-13):** Program "Harden the ADR-010 personalization layer — user isolation, crash safety, and real task execution" (Q1 Correctness, Q3 Reliability; the personalization design is `docs/adr/ADR-010-personalized-self-evolving-agents.md`'s "Feedback-as-fitness" per-user gardener registries and experience banks — a different decision from this log's own ADR-010), milestone 4/5. `internal/gardener/user_trees.go` carried two correctness bugs in the per-user gardener's evidence path. (1) `recordsForEntry` scored a personal tree's reflection evidence by strict-matching `evolution.Record.TreeName` against the registry entry's *display* `Name` — but `loadUserTreesLocked` disambiguates a colliding second-loaded entry by prefixing its display `Name` with `"<user>_"` while the tree's real ID (`entry.Tree.Name`) and every reflection `Record` recorded against it still carry the bare ID. A renamed entry therefore matched none of its own history — permanently evidence-starved at the gate — while the still-plain-named first entry matched by tree ID alone and silently absorbed *both* users' records, so two users owning trees with the same real tree ID scored on each other's feedback. (2) `bankFor` cached the shared `ExperienceBank` into `g.userBanks[entry.User]` whenever `evolution.NewExperienceBank` failed to open the per-user path — including transient, recoverable errors — permanently stranding that user on the shared bank even after the underlying problem cleared, since the cache hit on every later call short-circuited before a retry could happen.

**Decision:** `recordsForEntry` now resolves the match key from `entry.Tree.Name` (the tree's real ID) when set, falling back to `entry.Name` only when it isn't, so both the plain-named and the collision-renamed entry match on the same underlying identity. The matched records are then filtered to `r.User == "" || r.User == entry.User`, so two users' trees sharing one real tree ID no longer bleed evidence into each other while pre-Phase-5/seed records with no `User` attribution still count for either owner. `bankFor` no longer writes to `g.userBanks` on an open error — it returns the shared bank for that one call only, leaving the cache slot empty so the next call retries opening the user's own per-user path.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes milestone 4/5 of the ADR-010-personalization-hardening program; milestone 5 remains open.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A collision-renamed personal tree (`"<user>_<treeID>"`) is no longer evidence-starved — the evidence gate scores it against its own real reflection history instead of treating a renamed tree as perpetually unproven.
- ✅ Two users owning trees with the same real tree ID no longer contaminate each other's reflection scoring; a plain-named entry only sees its own owner's records plus unattributed legacy records.
- ✅ A transient per-user experience-bank open failure no longer permanently strands the user on the shared bank — the next `bankFor` call retries the user's own path instead of hitting a poisoned cache entry.
- Pinned by `TestRecordsForEntry_CollidingUsersFilterByRecordUser` (plain-named vs. collision-renamed entries, `internal/gardener/user_trees_test.go`) and `TestBankFor_TransientOpenErrorDoesNotPermanentlyCacheSharedBank` (blocks the per-user experience path with a regular file, asserts fallback, clears the block, asserts the next call no longer returns the shared bank).

---

## ADR-067: User-Scoped Runtime-Generated Tree Resolution (ADR-010 Personalization Hardening Program, milestone 3/5)

**Context (2026-07-13):** Program "Harden the ADR-010 personalization layer — user isolation, crash safety, and real task execution" (Q1 Correctness, Q3 Reliability). Runtime-generated tree IDs like `goal:automate_<slug>` are derived deterministically from the goal text, not from the requesting user, so two different users can independently compile a personal tree under the exact same ID. The existing fallback, `agentexec.resolveUserTree` (installed as `domains.DynamicResolveFn` via `agentexec.ResolveGeneratedTree`), resolves such an ID by scanning every `users/<id>/trees` workspace in sorted order and returning the first match — a collision therefore always resolves to whichever user's name sorts first, silently handing that user's personal tree to every other colliding user's request (bob asking for his automation could receive alice's tree, running her personalized behavior under his identity).

**Decision:** Add a parallel, user-scoped resolution path instead of changing the existing unscoped one (which other callers still rely on). `agentexec.ResolveGeneratedTreeForUser(user, id)` consults the shared reflections dir first, exactly like `ResolveGeneratedTree`, but its per-user fallback loads only `users/<user>/trees` — the one workspace matching the requesting user — never the sorted cross-user scan. `domains.DynamicResolveForUserFn` is a new nil-tolerant injection seam (mirroring `DynamicResolveFn`'s convention), wired to `ResolveGeneratedTreeForUser` in `agentexec.init` alongside the existing wiring. `domains.ResolveTreeIDForUser(user, id)` is the domains-level entry point: an empty `user` falls back to the unscoped `ResolveTreeID`; a non-empty one routes every `dynamicResolve` call site through the new seam via a `resolveTreeIDWithResolver(id, resolve)` helper — `resolveTreeID`'s existing ID-prefix branching (`finance:`, `research:`, `domain:`, `startup:`, `thinktank:`, the final runtime-generated fallback) is factored to take the resolver function as a parameter, so `ResolveTreeID` and `ResolveTreeIDForUser` share one branching implementation instead of two copies that could drift.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes milestone 3/5 of the ADR-010-personalization-hardening program (ADR-066 closed milestone 4/5 the same day; milestone 5 remains open).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Given a requesting user, `ResolveGeneratedTreeForUser`/`ResolveTreeIDForUser` can no longer resolve a slug-collided ID to a different user's tree — each user's own workspace is the only per-user fallback consulted.
- ✅ The unscoped `ResolveGeneratedTree`/`ResolveTreeID`/`DynamicResolveFn` path is untouched, so existing callers keep their current (collision-prone but unchanged) behavior rather than being silently altered.
- ⚠️ Not yet wired into production: `agentexec.NewRunDeps()` and `cmd/bt-agent/main.go`'s `resolveTree` — which feeds both `agent.RunDeps.ResolveTree` and `a2a_mod.SetTreeResolver` — still call the unscoped `domains.ResolveTreeID`, and `internal/agent/runner.go`'s `TreeResolver` type takes only an id, no user. The collision this ADR fixes is therefore still live on the real `goal:automate_*` request path until a follow-up milestone threads a requesting-user identity through `RunDeps`/`RunAgent` and switches the call site to `ResolveTreeIDForUser`. **Resolved by ADR-069 (2026-07-13):** `RunDeps`/`RunOnce` now consult the scoped resolver whenever the running agent has a known owner, closing milestone 5/5 of the program.
- Pinned by `TestResolveGeneratedTreeForUser_TwoUserCollision` and `TestResolveTreeIDForUser_ScopesToRequestingUser` (`internal/agentexec/wiring_test.go`), both of which save colliding same-ID trees for two users and assert each resolves their own.

---

## ADR-068: Distinguishing an Escalated-Origin HITL Resolution from a Routine Pending One (Closes the HITL-Escalation Program, Milestone 4/4)

**Context (2026-07-13):** Program "Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability — stop HITL escalation from silently auto-approving workflow steps," milestone 4/4. ADR-061 (milestones 1–3) made `FindPendingByTaskID` match `StatusEscalated` alongside `StatusPending`, so `ApproveByTaskID`/`RejectByTaskID` could resolve an escalated request by task ID — but the callers of those methods gave no sign of which case had actually happened. `handleTaskApprove`/`handleTaskReject` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`) returned the same `{"status": "approved"/"rejected", "id": ...}` shape regardless of whether the resolved request had been a routine pending approval or one a human operator had already escalated for review — and if `hitl.DefaultStore` held no matching request at all, the resulting error from `ApproveByTaskID`/`RejectByTaskID` was silently discarded, so the dashboard task still flipped to `"approved"`/`"rejected"` with no indication the HITL side had done nothing. The `bt_workflow_approve` MCP tool's by-task-ID branch (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) had the identical blind spot: it `json.Marshal`ed the resolved `*hitl.Request` verbatim with no signal of its originating status.

**Decision:** Both call sites now probe `hitl.DefaultStore.FindPendingByTaskID(taskID)` *before* calling `ApproveByTaskID`/`RejectByTaskID`, and derive `resolvedFrom := "pending"` unless the pre-resolution request's `Status == hitl.StatusEscalated`, in which case `resolvedFrom = "escalated"`. `handleTaskApprove`/`handleTaskReject` add `resp["hitl_resolved_from"] = resolvedFrom` on success, and — new — add `resp["hitl_note"] = err.Error()` on failure instead of swallowing the `hitl: no pending request for task %q` error from `hitl.DefaultStore`, so a task with no matching HITL request at all is now distinguishable in the response from one that resolved cleanly. `bt_workflow_approve`'s by-task-ID branch marshals an anonymous struct embedding `*hitl.Request` plus a `resolved_from` JSON field instead of the bare request, giving MCP callers the same signal REST callers now get.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes milestone 4/4; the "stop HITL escalation from silently auto-approving workflow steps" program is now fully landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A dashboard or MCP caller resolving a task by ID can now tell whether it was a routine pending approval or one that had already been escalated to a human, instead of the two cases being indistinguishable in the response.
- ✅ A task with no matching HITL request no longer silently reports `"approved"`/`"rejected"` on the dashboard REST path with zero indication the HITL side failed — the underlying `hitl.DefaultStore` error now surfaces as `hitl_note`.
- Pinned by `TestHandleTaskApproveReject_EscalatedVsPending` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`), a table-driven test covering approve/reject × pending/escalated/no-request.

---

## ADR-069: `agent.RunDeps`/`RunOnce` Consult a User-Scoped Tree Resolver, Closing the ADR-010 Personalization Hardening Program (Milestone 5/5)

**Context (2026-07-13):** NotebookLM research goal, and the explicit follow-up ADR-067 flagged in its own consequences: `agentexec.ResolveGeneratedTreeForUser`/`domains.ResolveTreeIDForUser` existed and were tested to resolve a slug-collided `goal:automate_<slug>` tree ID to the requesting user's own workspace instead of whichever colliding user's directory sorted first — but nothing on the real request path called them. `agent.RunDeps.ResolveTree` (`internal/agent/runner.go`) took only a tree ID, with no way to pass a requesting user through, and both production wiring sites — `cmd/bt-agent/main.go`'s `resolveTree` and `internal/agentexec/deps.go`'s `NewRunDeps()` — populated `RunDeps.ResolveTree` with the unscoped `domains.ResolveTreeID` alone. The collision ADR-067 fixed at the resolver level was therefore still fully live on the one path that matters: a scheduled personal automation's `RunOnce` call.

**Decision:** `internal/agent/runner.go` adds a `UserTreeResolver` type (`func(user, treeID string) *evolution.SerializableNode`) and a `RunDeps.ResolveTreeForUser` field of that type, alongside the existing unscoped `ResolveTree`. `RunOnce` resolves the agent's `Definition` first (as it already did), and if `def.Metadata["user"]` is non-empty *and* `ResolveTreeForUser` is set, it builds a closure over that owning user and uses it — instead of the bare `ResolveTree` — for both tree-ID lookups the function performs (the primary `treeID` resolve and the `agentName` fallback resolve). A nil `ResolveTreeForUser` or an unowned agent (`Metadata["user"] == ""`) falls back to the unscoped resolver unchanged, so existing unowned agents and any caller that never wires the new field keep current behavior exactly. `cmd/bt-agent/main.go` adds a `resolveTreeForUser(user, id string) *evolution.SerializableNode` wrapper over `domains.ResolveTreeIDForUser` and wires it into the daemon's `agent.RunDeps` literal; `internal/agentexec/deps.go`'s `NewRunDeps()` wires the equivalent closure for the Superpowers pipeline's in-process executor.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes milestone 5/5 of "Harden the ADR-010 personalization layer — user isolation, crash safety, and real task execution."

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A scheduled personal automation registered under a deterministic slug tree ID can no longer receive a different, alphabetically-earlier user's identically-slugged compiled tree — `RunOnce` now routes such a lookup through `ResolveTreeIDForUser` end to end on both production entry points (`bt-agent`'s daemon `RunDeps` and `agentexec`'s Superpowers-pipeline `RunDeps`).
- ✅ The scoping decision is made once, from data already in scope at `RunOnce` time (`Definition.Metadata["user"]`, set by `cmd/bt-agent`'s `activateAutomation`) — no new parameter threading through `RunAgent`/MCP tool signatures was needed.
- ✅ Unowned agents (no `Metadata["user"]`) and any `RunDeps` that leaves `ResolveTreeForUser` nil are unaffected — the unscoped `ResolveTree` path is exercised exactly as before.
- Pinned by `TestRunOnce_UsesUserScopedResolverWhenAgentHasOwner` (`internal/agent/runner_test.go`), which fails the test if the unscoped `ResolveTree` is consulted at all for an owned agent, and asserts the scoped resolver receives the correct user and tree ID.

---

## ADR-070: `NormalizeImpactSource` Exported from `internal/knowledge`, Closing Half of the CLI/MCP Normalization Split (Q1 Correctness Program, Milestone 1/3)

**Context (2026-07-13):** Program "Q1 Correctness — make `bt_impact_tests` honestly normalize and reject source paths like its CLI sibling," milestone 1/3. ADR-052 wired two consumers onto `knowledge.ImpactedTests`, but only the CLI subcommand got path normalization: `cmd/bt-agent-cli/impact.go`'s unexported `normalizeImpactSource` converted an absolute or cwd-relative path into the module-relative form the impact graph indexes and rejected anything outside `root`, while `cmd/bt-agent`'s `bt_impact_tests` MCP tool documented `source` as "already module-relative" and passed it straight to `knowledge.ImpactedTests` with no such conversion. Since `ImpactGraph.TestsFor` simply returns an empty slice for a key it doesn't recognize, an MCP caller that supplied an absolute path (or one relative to a different working directory) got a silent empty test list indistinguishable from "no tests are impacted," rather than either a correct lookup or an explicit error — and because the normalization logic lived only in the CLI package, `impact_tools.go` had no seam to reuse it.

**Decision:** Move `normalizeImpactSource`'s logic into `internal/knowledge/impact.go` as an exported `NormalizeImpactSource(root, source string) (string, error)`, alongside `ImpactedTests`, unchanged in behavior — abs-path resolution, module-relative conversion via `filepath.Rel`, and outside-root rejection. `cmd/bt-agent-cli/impact.go`'s `impactedTestsForSource` now calls `knowledge.NormalizeImpactSource` instead of a local copy, which is deleted. This milestone is a pure extract-and-export: `cmd/bt-agent/impact_tools.go`'s `impactTests` handler is untouched and still calls `knowledge.ImpactedTests` directly without normalizing `source` first, so the MCP tool's silent-empty-list gap for absolute-path input is not yet closed — that is milestones 2–3. Pinned by `TestNormalizeImpactSource_AbsoluteUnderRoot`/`TestNormalizeImpactSource_RejectsOutsideRoot` (`internal/knowledge/impact_test.go`, covering an absolute path under root, a plain relative path, and a path outside root), with the CLI package's own tests re-pointed at the shared function.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — milestone 1/3; milestones 2–3 (wiring `bt_impact_tests` to call `NormalizeImpactSource` and deciding how it should report an outside-root or malformed path) **resolved by ADR-071 (2026-07-13)**.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ The abs-path/module-relative/outside-root-rejection logic now has one home, `knowledge.NormalizeImpactSource`, instead of being reachable only from the CLI package — `cmd/bt-agent`'s MCP handler can now adopt it without duplicating the logic.
- ✅ `cmd/bt-agent-cli`'s observable behavior is unchanged — the same two cases (absolute-under-root, outside-root) are covered, now exercised through the shared package function instead of a local copy.
- ⚠️ `bt_impact_tests` still does not call `NormalizeImpactSource` — an MCP caller passing an absolute or differently-rooted path still gets a silent empty test list rather than a normalized lookup or an explicit rejection, the exact gap ADR-052 left open. **Closed by ADR-071 (2026-07-13):** `impactTests` now normalizes `source` before querying and returns `{"error": ...}` for a path outside `root`.
- Pinned by `TestNormalizeImpactSource_AbsoluteUnderRoot`/`TestNormalizeImpactSource_RejectsOutsideRoot` (`internal/knowledge/impact_test.go`).

---

## ADR-071: `bt_impact_tests` Normalizes and Honestly Rejects Out-of-Root Source Paths, Closing the Q1 Correctness Program (Milestone 3/3)

**Context (2026-07-13):** Program "Q1 Correctness — make `bt_impact_tests` honestly normalize and reject source paths like its CLI sibling," milestone 3/3, following ADR-070's milestone-1/3 extract-and-export of `knowledge.NormalizeImpactSource`. `cmd/bt-agent/impact_tools.go`'s `impactTests` still passed `source` straight to `knowledge.ImpactedTests` unnormalized: an MCP caller supplying an absolute path (or one relative to a different working directory than `root`) got a silent `{"tests": []}` — indistinguishable from "no tests are impacted" — instead of a correct lookup or an explicit error, while `cmd/bt-agent-cli`'s `impactedTestsForSource` (its sibling, already calling `NormalizeImpactSource` since ADR-070) honestly rejected the same input with an error.

**Decision:** `impactTests` now calls `knowledge.NormalizeImpactSource(root, source)` first and returns `{"error": ...}` immediately if it fails — the same shape `cmd/bt-agent-cli/impact.go`'s `impactedTestsForSource` already uses — before passing the normalized, module-relative result to `knowledge.ImpactedTests`. This is the same one-line composition as the CLI sibling, so the two callers of `NormalizeImpactSource` are now identical in shape as well as behavior. Pinned by `TestImpactTests_NormalizesAbsoluteSourceUnderRoot` (an absolute path under `root` now resolves to the same impacted-tests list a module-relative path would) and `TestImpactTests_RejectsSourceOutsideRoot` (a path outside `root` now returns `{"error": ...}` instead of `{"tests": []}`), both in `cmd/bt-agent/impact_tools_test.go`.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — milestone 3/3; closes the Q1 Correctness program opened by ADR-070.

**Consequences:**
