**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Deterministic coverage backlog: characterization tests for `cmd/benchcmp/main.go` and 2 more (Q1 Correctness)," milestones 2–3 of 3. `cmd/bt-agent/blackboard_tools.go` and `cmd/bt-agent/goal_tools.go` had no direct test files of their own; the milestone's mandate was to pin currently-observed behavior with new `blackboard_tools_test.go`/`goal_tools_test.go` files, table-driven where natural, and to touch production code only if a test exposed a real bug. Writing the tables exposed two.

**Decision:** Fix both minimally, in place, with no other behavior change.
- **`goalTreeSlug`** (`cmd/bt-agent/goal_tools.go`) compresses a goal name into the tree-ID fragment used by `"goal:" + goalTreeSlug(goal.Name)` (autopilot tree IDs), MCP-tool task IDs, and feedback task IDs. It built the underscore-joined slug by writing a separator whenever a field's *index* was `>0`, not whenever the previous field actually contributed characters — so a field that filtered down to nothing (pure punctuation, e.g. `"watch & alert!"`'s `"&"` field) still triggered a separator, producing a stray/doubled underscore (`"watch__alert"` instead of `"watch_alert"`). The fix accumulates each field into a scratch `strings.Builder` first and only appends it — with a preceding separator if the output so far is non-empty — when that field actually produced at least one character, so an empty field contributes nothing at all instead of an orphan separator.
- **`scopedStore.get`** (`internal/blackboard/memory_store.go`) — see §8.4.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestones 2 and 3 of 3; milestone 1 (`cmd/benchcmp/main.go`) covered separately.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Goal names with trailing punctuation or symbol-only words no longer mint tree/task IDs with stray double underscores, which previously risked two distinct goal names slugging to visually-confusable-but-distinct IDs and made generated IDs harder to read.
- ✅ `bt_bb_read` now agrees with `bt_bb_write`/`bt_bb_delete`/`bt_bb_list` on whitespace/slash-padded keys, closing the one asymmetric path in the `scopedStore` key contract.
- Pinned by `TestGoalTreeSlug` (`cmd/bt-agent/goal_tools_test.go`, table-driven, including a `"strips punctuation"` case asserting `"watch & alert!"` → `"watch_alert"`) and `TestBTBBWriteThenReadNormalizesKeyIdentically` (`cmd/bt-agent/blackboard_tools_test.go`); both new test files otherwise characterize the pre-existing behavior of their respective tool files with no further production changes.

## ADR-093: Durable Pre-Mutation Snapshots and Operator-Triggered Tree Rollback for the Gardener

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Wire durable pre-mutation snapshots, tree rollback, and benchmark-gated winner acceptance into the gardener and evolve tools," milestones 1–3 of 3 (benchmark-gated acceptance itself — `QuickValidate`/`QualityGate.ValidateFor` — already existed and is unchanged by this ADR). `gardener.Config.SnapshotDir` (`internal/gardener/gardener.go:425`) was already populated in production (`cmd/bt-gardener/config.go:79`, `snapDir := filepath.Join(metricsDir, "snapshots")`) and `evolution.SnapshotTree`/`RestoreTree` (`internal/evolution/quality_gate.go:143`/`:167`) already existed, but nothing called either: the pre-mutation tree state lived only in `evolveTreeV2`'s in-memory `originalTree := cloneTreeForGardener(tree)` clone, used solely for that same cycle's own ACCEPT/ROLLBACK comparison (§6.2/§8.5) — a process crash mid-cycle lost the pre-mutation state entirely, and there was no way to recover a bad mutation already committed by a *previous* cycle without hand-editing the tree file.

**Decision:** Wire the existing snapshot/restore primitives into the live gardener path. **Milestone 1:** `evolveTreeV2` calls `evolution.SnapshotTree(tree, entry.Name, g.cfg.SnapshotDir)` immediately before the mutation loop begins (right before `originalTree` is cloned), when `SnapshotDir` is configured; a snapshot failure logs a warning and does not abort the cycle. **Milestone 2:** a new `Registry.RollbackTree(name, snapshotDir string) error` (`internal/gardener/gardener.go`) calls `evolution.RestoreTree(name, snapshotDir)`, swaps the matching registry entry's in-memory `Tree` under `r.mu`, and durably persists it via the existing `SaveTree` — returning an error rather than silently no-op'ing when `name` has no registry entry. **Milestone 3:** a new `GardenerRollbackTool` (`cmd/bt-gardener/main.go`), mirroring the existing `GardenerStatusTool`/`GardenerRunCycleTool` langchain-tool pattern, parses a tree name from its input and calls `Registry.RollbackTree`, registered as `gardener_rollback` alongside the three pre-existing tools (status/run_cycle/recommend) and described in the agent's tool prompt — so recovery is operator- or LLM-triggerable instead of requiring code changes.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestones 1–3 of 3 landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A process crash mid-cycle no longer loses the pre-mutation tree state: it now survives on disk at `~/.go-bt-gardener/snapshots/snapshot_<name>.json`, independent of the in-memory `originalTree` clone the same cycle's own ACCEPT/ROLLBACK logic uses
- ✅ A bad mutation already committed and saved by a prior cycle can be recovered via `gardener_rollback` without rerunning a full evolution cycle
- ⚠️ The snapshot is a single slot per tree, overwritten (tmp+rename) at the start of every cycle — it always holds only the *most recent* cycle's pre-mutation state, not a history; rolling back after N accepted cycles only undoes the most recent one, not all N
- ⚠️ `RollbackTree` restores unconditionally — it does not re-run `QualityGate.ValidateFor`/`MetaValidator.ValidateMutation` against the restored tree, nor does it reset a tree's `QualityGate` fail-streak (`ResetFailCount`/`IsDisabledFor`) if the mutation that made rollback necessary had already disabled the gate for that tree
- Pinned by `TestEvolveTreeV2_SnapshotsTreeBeforeMutation`, `TestRegistry_RollbackTree`, `TestRegistry_RollbackTree_UnknownTree` (`internal/gardener`), and `TestGardenerRollbackTool_CallRestoresSnapshot` (`cmd/bt-gardener`)

## ADR-094: Gardener-Embedded Transposition Table and Deterministic Eviction (Q2 Evolvability, Milestones 1–3/3)

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Wire the Stockfish transposition-table search into the gardener's live evolution loop." `evaluator.TranspositionTable` (`internal/evaluator/stockfish.go`) already cached `(tree, task)` fitness evaluations, but its only production caller was the standalone `bt-evaluator` MCP binary — the gardener's own `RunCycleV2` never constructed, stored into, or persisted one, so cached evaluations were lost on every gardener restart and re-computed from scratch even when `bt-evaluator` had already scored the same position. Eviction was also non-deterministic: `Store`'s over-capacity path and `load`'s startup trim both deleted whatever entry Go's randomized map iteration visited first.

**Decision:** Thread a `*evaluator.TranspositionTable` through the gardener's own cycle instead of leaving it exclusive to `bt-evaluator`. A new `Config.TranspositionTablePath` backs a lazily-constructed, `Gardener`-cached table (`transpositionTable()`, `internal/gardener/evolve_v2.go`); `evolveTreeV2` stores this cycle's evaluation into it right after computing `baseFitness` — ahead of the evidence gate, so every processed tree contributes regardless of downstream short-circuits — and `RunCycleV2` saves the table after every tree, alongside the existing `MetricsTracker.Save()` call. `CycleMetrics` gains `DeepSearchUsed`/`DeepSearchDepth`/`TTHitRate`, populated from `evaluator.IterativeDeepening`'s `DeepeningResult` when a table is configured (metrics-only — the deep-search result does not yet bias mutation selection). Eviction in `TranspositionTable.Store`/`load` is now deterministic: `TranspositionEntry` gains an `InsertedAt` marker stamped from a monotonic per-table sequence counter on every `Store`, and a new `evictLowestValueEntry` helper always removes the least-recently-written entry instead of a random one.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestones 1–3 of 3 landed. `TranspositionTablePath` is not yet set by `cmd/bt-gardener/config.go`, so the daemon's production cycles do not populate or persist a table yet — this is analogous to `Config.SelectorStatsPath` before ADR-079 wired it into `buildGardenerConfig`.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Once `TranspositionTablePath` is wired into a production entry point, cached `(tree, task)` evaluations survive gardener restarts instead of resetting with every process cycle, closing the gap between the gardener's own evolution loop and the persistence `bt-evaluator` already had.
- ✅ Cache eviction is now reproducible: given the same sequence of `Store` calls, the same entry is always evicted, which makes eviction behavior testable (`TestTranspositionTable_Store_EvictsLowestValueDeterministically`) where the prior random-map-order deletion was not.
- ⚠️ Eviction picks the least-recently-*written* entry, not the lowest-fitness one — a high-value entry written once early and never overwritten is evicted before a low-value entry rewritten more recently; `Probe` (a read) does not refresh `InsertedAt`, so this is closer to FIFO-by-write than true LRU.
- ⚠️ `IterativeDeepening`'s result is recorded in `CycleMetrics` but not yet consulted by the mutation-acceptance path — deep search runs and reports, but doesn't (yet) change which mutation the cycle applies.
- ⚠️ No production binary sets `Config.TranspositionTablePath` yet, so milestones 1–2 are reachable only from tests until a follow-up wires it into `cmd/bt-gardener/config.go`.
- Pinned by `TestRunCycleV2_TranspositionTablePersistsAcrossGardenerInstances` (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2_test.go`); `TestEvolveTreeV2_DeepSearchMetrics_PopulatedWhenTTConfigured`, `TestEvolveTreeV2_DeepSearchMetrics_ZeroWithoutTranspositionTable` (`internal/gardener/gardener_test.go`); and `TestTranspositionTable_StoreProbeRoundTrip`, `TestTranspositionTable_StoreProbeMiss`, `TestTranspositionTable_SaveLoadRoundTrip`, `TestTranspositionTable_Store_EvictsLowestValueDeterministically`, `TestTranspositionTable_Store_EvictsLowestValueRepeated`, `TestTranspositionTable_Store_EvictionPreservesLowestAmongSurvivors` (`internal/evaluator/stockfish_test.go`)

## ADR-095: A Durable, Learning Archive for Expert Knowledge (Q2 Evolvability, Milestones 1–2/2)

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Give Expert Knowledge a durable, learning archive instead of a static hardcoded rule set." `ExpertKnowledge` (`internal/evolution/expert.go`) had shipped as a fixed, code-defined catalog — 6 design `Patterns`, 5 `AntiPatterns`, `Heuristics`, `TreeArchetypes` — consulted by `MetaValidator` (ADR-088) and surfaced advisory-only through `bt_evolve_expert`'s `RecommendMutations`/`DetectAntiPatterns`. Unlike every other evolution algorithm in the package (`QTable`/ADR-041, `IslandModel`/ADR-033, `MAPElitesGrid`/ADR-033, `ParetoFront`/ADR-091), it had no durable state and could not grow from what real evolution runs actually observed: a mutation `bt_evolve_qlearning` found to genuinely improve fitness left no trace `ExpertKnowledge` could later draw on.

**Decision:** Give `ExpertKnowledge` the same durable-archive idiom the other algorithms use, then thread it through the one production path that already computes a fitness delta per mutation. **Milestone 1:** a new `ExpertKnowledge.LearnedPatterns []LearnedPattern` field (`{Action, Category string; Gain float64}`) accumulates via a new `Observe(action, category string, gain float64)` method — nil-safe (a `nil *ExpertKnowledge` is a no-op) and gain-gated (`gain <= 0` is discarded, so only genuine improvements are retained). `Population.EvolveQLearning` (`internal/evolution/learning.go:846`) and its per-mutation helper `qLearnMutate` (`learning.go:887-924`) both gain an additional `ek *ExpertKnowledge` parameter; `qLearnMutate` calls `ek.Observe(action, category, after-before)` right after computing the same `after-before` delta it already feeds into `qt.Update`, so `ExpertKnowledge` and the `QTable` learn from the identical signal. `ExpertKnowledge.Save`/`Load` (mirroring `QTable.Save`/`Load`) persist `LearnedPatterns` alone (the hardcoded catalog fields are rebuilt fresh by `NewExpertKnowledge` every call and are deliberately not persisted) as JSON via atomic tmp+rename under the ADR-024 `acquireExperienceLock` flock; `Load` appends rather than clobbers, and a missing archive is a silent cold start. **Milestone 2:** a new `expertArchivePath(treeID string) string` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) mirrors `qtableArchivePath`'s per-base-tree scoping and sanitization (ADR-034). `bt_evolve_qlearning` constructs an `ExpertKnowledge`, loads it from this path before calling `EvolveQLearning`, and saves it back after — both non-fatal on error (`expert_archive_load_error`/`expert_archive_save_error`), matching the QTable archive's degrade-to-cold-start behavior in the same handler. `bt_evolve_expert` loads the same archive before calling `RecommendMutations`/`DetectAntiPatterns` and returns the merged `learned_patterns` in its JSON result (plus a non-fatal `expert_archive_load_error`), so an advisory call surfaces accumulated cross-run learned patterns alongside the static catalog rather than the catalog alone.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestones 1–2 of 2 landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `ExpertKnowledge` is no longer purely static: every `bt_evolve_qlearning` call that finds a genuinely fitness-improving mutation grows the same durable archive `bt_evolve_expert` reads, closing the one evolution algorithm in the package that had no cross-run memory at all.
- ✅ Reuses the ADR-024 flock, the ADR-034 sanitization helper, and the QTable's `Save`/`Load` cold-start/corrupt-archive contract rather than inventing new persistence semantics.
- ✅ Nil-safe `Observe` means every other existing `EvolveQLearning`/`qLearnMutate` caller (tests, and any future caller that doesn't care about `ExpertKnowledge`) is unaffected by the new parameter beyond having to pass `nil`.
- ⚠️ Unlike `QTable`/`IslandModel`/`MAPElitesGrid`/`ParetoFront`, `ExpertKnowledge`'s archive has no `Cap` — `LearnedPatterns` grows unbounded across repeated `bt_evolve_qlearning` calls against one base tree, the same unbounded state the QTable itself shipped with before ADR-041 milestone 4 closed the gap.
- ⚠️ `LearnedPatterns` is observed only from `EvolveQLearning`'s mutation loop — the other five evolution algorithms (`Evolve`, `EvolveWithExperience`, `EvolvePareto`, `EvolveAll`, `EvolveMAPElites`, `NSGAIIPopulation.Evolve`) do not call `Observe`, so the learned archive reflects only what `bt_evolve_qlearning` itself has explored, not the fleet's full mutation history.
- ⚠️ `RecommendMutations`/`DetectAntiPatterns` still score and rank using only the hardcoded `Patterns`/`AntiPatterns` catalog — `LearnedPatterns` is surfaced in `bt_evolve_expert`'s JSON response for a caller to read, but does not yet feed back into which recommendations are generated or how they're ranked.
- Pinned by `TestExpertKnowledge_ObservesLearnedPatternFromQLearning` and `TestPopulation_EvolveQLearning_NilExpertKnowledgeNoOp` (`internal/evolution/learning_test.go`) and `TestBTEvolveExpertSurfacesLearnedPatternFromQLearning` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`).

## ADR-096: Benchmark-Suite Gate on the Standalone Evolution Tools' Durable-Archive Winners (Q2 Evolvability, Milestones 1–2/4)

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Gate the standalone evolution-algorithm tools' durable-archive winners through the benchmark suite, not structural fitness alone." Every `bt_evolve_*` tool that persists a winner to a durable cross-run archive (`bt_evolve_multiobjective`/ADR-091, `bt_evolve_island`/ADR-033, and — pending milestones 3–4 — `bt_evolve_pareto`, `bt_evolve_qlearning`) picks that winner using only `evolution.StructuralMultiFitness`/its siblings — success_rate/node_efficiency/stability computed from tree shape via structural heuristics, never an actual execution. A mutation can score as structurally elite while performing worse than the untouched base tree against the tree's own `internal/benchmark` suite; the "Mutation quality gates" Known Technical Debt row (§11) already flagged this exact gap for the daemon's `bt_evolve_*` tools after ADR-088 closed the analogous gap for the gardener's `evolveTreeV2`.

**Decision (milestone 1 of 4):** Add `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner(treeID string, base, winner *evolution.SerializableNode) (rejected bool, baseRate, winnerRate float64)` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`). It runs both the base tree and the winner through `benchmark.SuiteForTree(treeID)` with a `Sandbox`-mode mock LLM via `benchmark.RunSuite` — deterministic, no real LLM calls, `-short`-safe, preserving the ADR-009 determinism guarantee for these tools — called through a new package-level `benchmarkRunSuiteFn` seam (mirroring the existing `DelegateToA2AFn`/`AuctionDelegateFn` test-override pattern), and reports `rejected=true` when the winner's `SuccessRate` is lower than the base tree's. `bt_evolve_multiobjective` calls it on the NSGA-II front's lead individual immediately before `nsga.Save(archivePath)`: on rejection the save is skipped entirely (the durable NSGA-II archive, ADR-091, is left untouched), and the JSON result always carries `benchmark_gate_rejected`/`benchmark_base_success_rate`/`benchmark_winner_success_rate` so a caller can see the gate's verdict either way.

**Decision (milestone 2 of 4):** Reuse the same `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner`/`benchmarkRunSuiteFn` seam for `bt_evolve_island`. `IslandModel.EvolveAll`'s return value — a `map[string]*evolution.SerializableNode` of each domain's best tree for that generation — was previously discarded by the handler's generation loop (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`); the loop now captures the final generation's map, and the handler picks a "cross-island best individual" as the tree belonging to whichever *seeded* domain has the highest `perIslandBest` structural fitness this run (a run with no seeded domain scoring at all skips the gate rather than gating a nil tree). That winner is gated against `baseTree` immediately before `im.Save(archivePath)`, with the identical unconditional-result/skip-save-on-reject contract as milestone 1: `benchmark_gate_rejected`/`benchmark_base_success_rate`/`benchmark_winner_success_rate` are always set, and `im.Save` runs only when the gate does not reject.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestones 1 and 2 of 4 landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `bt_evolve_multiobjective` and `bt_evolve_island` can no longer persist a structurally-elite-but-behaviorally-regressed winner into their durable archives — the first two of the four `bt_evolve_*` tools with a durable archive to be checked against real execution behavior rather than a structural proxy alone.
- ✅ `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner`/`benchmarkRunSuiteFn` were written in milestone 1 to be reused verbatim, and milestone 2 confirms that: `bt_evolve_island`'s wiring adds no new gating logic, only the cross-island-winner selection that feeds it.
- ⚠️ Both NSGA-II's and `IslandModel`'s mutation use unseeded `math/rand`, so an ordinary (non-adversarial) run can also regress and trip the gate — by design, but it means `benchmark_gate_rejected: true` can surface on an ordinary call, not only a contrived worst case, and any test asserting unconditional archive persistence must pin `benchmarkRunSuiteFn` to a non-regressing stub to stay deterministic (done for `TestBTEvolveMultiObjectiveAccumulatesDurableArchive` and, since milestone 2, every pre-existing `bt_evolve_island` archive-accumulation test — `TestBTEvolveIslandAccumulatesDurableArchive`, `TestBTEvolveIslandArchiveIsScopedPerBaseTree`, `TestBTEvolveIslandCapsBoundDurableArchiveAcrossCalls`, `TestBTEvolveIslandSurfacesEvictionCounters`, `TestBTEvolveIslandAdoptsLegacyGlobalArchiveOnce`).
- ⚠️ Milestones 3–4 (`bt_evolve_pareto`, `bt_evolve_qlearning`) remain open — those two tools still persist their durable archives on structural fitness alone.
- Pinned by `TestBTEvolveMultiObjectiveBenchmarkGateRejectsRegressedWinner` and, since milestone 2, `TestBTEvolveIslandBenchmarkGateRejectsRegressedWinner` (both `cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`).

# arc42 Section 10 — Quality Requirements

## 10.1 Quality Tree

```
go-bt-evolve
├── #reliable
│   ├── Panic recovery: SafeGo on all goroutines, tree-level defer/recover
│   ├── Circuit breaker: 3-state (closed/open/half-open), per-agent, configurable threshold
│   ├── Retry with backoff: Full jitter, 3 classes (standard, LLM, unknown), max 3 retries
│   └── Dead letter queue: Persistent JSON, exhausted retries preserved
├── #evolvable
│   ├── 6 evolution algorithms: Stockfish, Pareto, MAP-Elites, Island, Q-Learning, Expert
│   ├── Git-versioned trees: Every accepted mutation is a commit
│   ├── Benchmark gating: Pre/post fitness comparison with rollback
│   └── 10 mutation operators: add_before, add_after, wrap_retry, prune, swap_children, etc.
├── #secure
│   ├── Rate limiting: Token bucket, configurable rate
│   ├── API key auth: Bearer token validation on MCP and HTTP
│   ├── IP filtering: Allowlist/blocklist with CIDR support
│   ├── CSRF protection: Double-submit cookie pattern
│   ├── Security headers: X-Content-Type-Options, X-Frame-Options, etc.
│   ├── Audit logging: Request/response logging with dedup
│   └── Key rotation: Periodic API key refresh
├── #testable
│   ├── 295 test files across all packages
│   ├── 24+ passing packages (go test ./...)
│   ├── 78% average coverage (aspirational target: 85%)
│   ├── Test Watchdog cron: Detects new failures within 4h
│   └── Benchmark suite: BFCL, BTPG, ToolBench, SWE-bench integrations
├── #operable
│   ├── slog structured logging: JSON format, levels (debug/info/warn/error)
│   ├── Prometheus metrics: counters, gauges, full histogram series (_bucket/_sum/_count) + bt_build_info on /metrics
│   ├── Health endpoint: LLM availability check via bt_health
│   ├── Trace reader: OpenTelemetry spans with console tracer
│   └── Dashboard: 8-tab web UI on :9800
└── #flexible
    ├── 55+ trees across 8 categories: domain (23), finance (23), research, startup, thinktank, evolution, composed, core
    ├── 21-path merged main tree
    ├── 10 chain types: llm_call, agent, rag_query, tool_call, structured_output, refine, map_reduce, conversation, retrieval_qa, tool_action
    └── YAML-defined agents: Easy creation, templating, import/export
```

## 10.2 Quality Scenarios

| # | Scenario | Stimulus | Response | Measure |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| QS1 | Agent process crash | Goroutine panic in ChainAction | SafeGo recovers in <1s, DLQ persists task, circuit breaker opens for agent | Recovery <1s, no process restart needed |
| QS2 | 100 consecutive evolutions | bt-gardener runs evolution cycle | No fitness drop >20% from baseline | Fitness delta tracked per-mutation (aspirational) |
| QS3 | Dashboard tree listing | GET /api/tree with 55+ trees | Returns all trees with metadata | Response <500ms |
| QS4 | Test regression detection | New test failure introduced | Test Watchdog cron detects within 4h | Detection latency <4h |
| QS5 | Concurrent MCP calls | 3 simultaneous bt_run_task | bt-agent handles all 3 without deadlock | All 3 complete within timeout |
| QS6 | Ollama outage | LLM health check fails | All LLM-dependent tools return degraded error, non-LLM tools continue | Graceful degradation, no crashes |
| QS7 | Disk full during persistence | writeFile fails with ENOSPC | Error logged, operation returns failure, no corruption (atomic write aborted) | No partial/corrupt files |
| QS8 | Config validation | Invalid config.yaml on startup | Load fails with clear error message, defaults used as fallback | Config validation error reported |

# arc42 Section 11 — Risks and Technical Debt

## Prioritized Risk Table

| ID | Severity | Risk | Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | **RESOLVED** (2026-07-03) | ~~Mutation Death Spiral~~ — gardener overhaul: sandboxed benchmark cycles (Blackboard.Sandbox), per-tree quality gates (ValidateFor/IsDisabledFor), persisted trees loaded over builtins, rescaled gate floor. First fixed cycle: 22/50 trees mutated, 13 improved. | Evolution is productive again; post-convergence plateaus expected until fresh reflections arrive. | Keep per-tree metrics; feed new reflection records. |
| R2 | **HIGH** | **Single Point of Failure** — bt-agent is the sole task execution path for MCP tools. If it crashes or hangs, all Hermes Agent BT operations fail. | Dashboard sprints, cron jobs, and manual task delegation all blocked. | Add worker pool for horizontal scaling. Implement health-aware load shedding in gateway. **In progress (2026-07-05, ADR-011/ADR-012), routing adoption closed (2026-07-13, ADR-075):** the daemon constructs the `RemoteExecutor` + `AgentRouter` substrate from the live A2A card registry (`NewRouterFromEndpoints`), the dashboard `/api/scalability` endpoint and `bt-scalability-probe` read/drive it (milestones 3–4), and the scheduler's `AgentRunner` closure plus the DLQ replay executor now dispatch through it (milestone 5) instead of calling `agentRunner.RunOnce` directly — so a peer joining the registry can actually receive scheduled or replayed work; still single-node behavior until a peer card is present. |
| R3 | **MEDIUM** | **Dead Code** — Graphify reports 327 isolated nodes (no edges to the main graph). Dangling functions, unused types, dead test helpers. | Binary bloat, confusing codebase navigation, wasted maintenance effort. | Dead Code Sweeper cron removes isolated nodes weekly. Graphify community analysis flags candidates. |
| R4 | **MEDIUM** | **Package Sprawl** — 36 packages for ~136 source files (3.8 files per package). Many packages are thin wrappers (2-3 files). | Import complexity, circular dependency risk, harder to understand boundaries. | Consolidate to ~22 packages. Merge thin packages into domain-coherent groups. |
| R5 | **MEDIUM** | **Dashboard Untested** — 910-line `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go` with 0 dedicated tests. Pipeline handlers, task CRUD, and agent management have no test coverage. | Dashboard bugs go undetected until manual testing. Sprint failures are silent. | Add handler tests for all API endpoints. Add integration tests for sprint execution. |
| R6 | **MEDIUM** | **MCP + A2A Duplication** — Two separate server implementations (MCP stdio vs A2A HTTP) with overlapping auth, rate limiting, and tool registration. | Duplicated security logic, inconsistent behavior between protocols. | Extract shared server base. Unify auth, rate limiting, and middleware. |
| R7 | **LOW** | **DeepSeek API Dependency** — Escalation path depends on external API (api.deepseek.com). Outage or rate limiting blocks batch LLM work. **Note (2026-07-13, ADR-084):** `EscalateToDeepSeek` previously made no LLM call at all (a stub returning success unconditionally); it now performs a real call through whichever provider `bb.LLM`/`LLMProvider` config selects (deepseek/ollama/openrouter/acp), so this risk is now materialized rather than aspirational, and only actually hits `api.deepseek.com` when DeepSeek is the configured provider. | Batch processing delayed. Local Ollama is always available as fallback. | Monitor API health. Keep Ollama as always-available fallback. Consider additional providers. |
| R8 | **LOW** | **Evolution Engine Sprawl** — 13 graphify communities for evolution code. Overlapping strategies between Stockfish and Pareto causing redundant optimization. | Harder to reason about which algorithm applies when. Maintenance burden. | Strategy interface consolidation. Unify common pipeline stages across algorithms. |

### New Risks (2026-07-04)

| ID | Severity | Risk | Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R9 | **MEDIUM** | **Notebook corpus pollution** — weeks of self-description web research imported junk sources into the 334-source literature notebook. | Research answers cite junk; grill quality degraded. | Prune junk sources (needs human judgment on which of the 334 are legitimate); researcher now derives real queries. |
| R10 | **MEDIUM** | **Self-improvement navel-gazing** — with the literature source offline, all goals came from reviewing the loop's own commits; a full night landed only pipeline meta-improvements. | Platform capabilities stagnate while the pipeline polishes itself. | Structure-mode prompts steer to platform packages; seeded auction-allocation program; NotebookLM restored 2026-07-04. |
| R11 | **LOW** | **Loop-authored dormant scaffolding** — code written by the loop but never production-executed can hide landmines that only arm when wired (preflight cycle driver failed on first real execution). | Fleet-wide fast failures after wiring changes. | Contract tests at the binary level (cmd/bt-agent wiring test); arm dormant nodes with a manual verification run. |
| R12 | **LOW** | **Worktree/DLQ growth** — failed-run worktrees (24h grace) and the dead-letter queue accumulate. | Disk pressure on /tmp (1.9GB observed), noisy forensics. | Sweeper reaps >24h worktrees; DLQ growth now bounded — capped at `MaxDeadLetterEntries` (1000) with oldest-first eviction plus poison-pill abandonment (ADR-025). |

### New Risks (2026-07-08)

| ID | Severity | Risk | Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R13 | **MEDIUM** | **Stale daemon binary drift** — the long-running processes (`bt-agent.service`, bt-gardener, the gateway-spawned MCP children) keep serving code older than repo HEAD after autonomous landings; three incidents to date were diagnosed only by DLQ-message text heuristics (e.g. HALT text missing a fixed phrase). | Fixed behavior ships but does not run; recurring dead-letters are misattributed to code that is already fixed; triage is slow and heuristic. | Build identity embedded (ADR-023): startup log line + single-series `bt_build_info{revision,dirty}` gauge make the running revision directly comparable against repo HEAD. **Automated WARN detection live (2026-07-12, ADR-044, program 94b0b31 milestone 1/5):** `DriftStatus` compares the running revision against repo HEAD on a 20-minute background cadence and at every scheduler cycle, dead letters and webhook events carry `build_revision`. **All 5 milestones now landed (2026-07-12, ADR-045):** out-of-place rebuild + `AutoRebuild` wiring into all three mains (milestones 2–3), a `bt-dashboard`-specific rebuild target so it can swap its own binary, and a `RebuildBackoff` guard against retry-storming a broken HEAD (milestones 4–5). **Guardrails wired into production (2026-07-12, ADR-050):** all three mains now construct their `DriftWatchConfig` with `agent.NewRebuildBackoff()`, and `bt-agent` additionally sets `InFlightFn: globalSched.AnyInFlight` (the only one of the three that owns a live `Scheduler`) — a broken HEAD can no longer retry-storm rebuilds in production, and `bt-agent` will no longer swap its own binary out from under a mid-execution scheduled job. Residual: adopting a rebuilt binary still needs a process restart nothing here triggers, and `bt-dashboard`/`bt-gardener` have no equivalent in-flight guard since neither owns a `Scheduler`. |

## Known Technical Debt

| Debt Item | Status | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| Duplicate utility functions (strings, maps, template) | **Fixed** (2026-05-31) | Extracted to `internal/util/` with comprehensive tests (90.5% coverage) |
| Silent error suppression in test helpers | **Fixed** (2026-05-31) | All `_ = err` patterns replaced with explicit checks |
| DefaultTree god node (750+ lines) | **Fixed** (2026-05-31) | Extracted to 21-path merged tree across 7 category files |
| max_tokens audit (nodes with max_tokens=1) | **Aspirational** | Identify and fix ChainAction nodes with insufficient token budgets |
| Mutation quality gates | **Partially Fixed** (2026-07-14, ADR-088; 2026-07-15, ADR-096) | `evolution.MetaValidator`'s `checkFitness`/`checkStructuralRegression` compare pre/post composite fitness and baseline structural size against the candidate, and are now consulted inside the gardener's live `evolveTreeV2` acceptance loop — gardener cycles only. Since ADR-096 (Q2 Evolvability, milestones 1–2/4), `bt_evolve_multiobjective` and `bt_evolve_island` also gate their durable-archive winner through the real `internal/benchmark` suite (`benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner`) rather than structural fitness alone before persisting; `bt_evolve_pareto`/`bt_evolve_qlearning` still persist their durable archives on structural fitness alone (milestones 3–4 open) |
| Dead Code Sweeper cron | **Aspirational** | Weekly cron to detect and remove isolated code |

# arc42 Section 12 — Glossary

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **A2A** | Agent-to-Agent protocol. Enables agents to discover each other via agent cards and delegate tasks. Runs on HTTP :8686. |
| **Action** | A leaf node in a behavior tree that performs a side-effecting operation (read file, call LLM, write output). Returns Success (1) or Failure (0). |
| **ADR** | Architecture Decision Record. Documents a significant design choice with context, decision, and consequences. Immutable once accepted. |
| **Agent Card Signing** | An unkeyed SHA-256 content-hash integrity check over an `a2a.AgentCard` (`SignAgentCard`/`VerifyAgentCard`, `internal/a2a/signing.go`) — detects tampering or staleness, not origin (any party can compute a valid hash over their own forged card). Since 2026-07-14 (ADR-090), both card-producing paths sign: `ConvertToAgentCard` (`internal/a2a/card.go`, the origin point every registered-agent card passes through) and `handleGlobalAgentCard`'s ad hoc `/.well-known/agent-card.json` response (`internal/a2a/server.go`). `auctionCandidates` (`internal/a2a/auction.go`) — the trust boundary turning the live card registry into `AuctionDelegate`'s dispatchable candidates — checks it via `cardSignatureValid`: an unsigned card is trusted (signing is opt-in), but a card whose trailing signature no longer verifies against its content is excluded before `EligibleBidders` ever sees it. |
| **Auction (Contract Net)** | A2A task-allocation protocol: an Auctioneer announces a task, candidate agents bid from their agent-card capabilities, and the lowest-cost eligible bid is awarded and dispatched the work. `Auctioneer.RunAuction`, `internal/a2a/auction.go`. Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-064), winner dispatch is honest (`SendTask` returns a real error for any non-success outcome instead of formatting it as a success string) and resilient (bounded retry, then a per-winner `reliability.CircuitBreaker`, threshold 3/cooldown 30s). Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-065), production dispatches through `NewPersistentAuctioneer`, so that breaker survives across engine ticks (not just repeated calls on one `Auctioneer` instance, which production never made) and across restarts, round-tripped through `circuit_breakers.json`. |
| **Award** | The auction outcome naming the winning bidder and its winning Bid for an announced task. Produced by ScoreEvaluator. |
| **Bid** | A candidate agent's offer to perform an announced task, carrying cost and confidence scored from its agent card via ScoreAnnouncement. Returned by the bid-aware A2A responder. |
| **Blackboard** | Shared state object passed through behavior tree ticks. Carries Task, Plan, Result, Outcome, ChainState, ChainTools, Reflections, TreeStore. |
| **Build Identity** | The VCS identity a binary was built from (revision, commit time, dirty flag), read via `runtime/debug.ReadBuildInfo`. Logged at startup by bt-agent and bt-gardener and exposed as the single-series `bt_build_info{revision,dirty}` gauge on the metrics exposition; unstamped builds report an `unknown` sentinel instead of an empty label. Stale-daemon-binary drift is detected by comparing this revision against repo HEAD — automated via `DriftStatus` (ADR-044) on a 20-minute background cadence and at every scheduler cycle, and stamped as `build_revision` onto dead letters and webhook events. ADR-023, ADR-044. |
| **BuildTree** | Converts a SerializableNode tree definition into a runnable go-bt Command tree. Validates structure before building. |
| **ChainAction** | A behavior tree leaf node that wraps an LLM call. 10 chain types available. Reads config from node Name and Metadata. |
| **Chain Type** | One of 10 LLM workflow patterns: llm_call, agent, rag_query, tool_call, structured_output, refine, map_reduce, conversation, retrieval_qa, tool_action. |
| **Circuit Breaker** | 3-state pattern (closed/open/half-open) that prevents cascading failures. Per-agent isolation via AgentCircuitBreakerStore, now durable: `Save`/`Load` (`internal/agent/scheduler_cb.go`) persist and restore state through `circuit_breakers.json`, written on every `runJob` outcome and restored on `NewScheduler` — the file the dashboard's `cb_status` column has always read but that, before ADR-063 (2026-07-13), no writer ever populated. Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-054), `ACPClient` also carries its own `reliability.CircuitBreaker` keyed by ACP command, tripped by repeated subprocess-start/session-setup failures so a crash-looping ACP subprocess is not respawned on every `GenerateCtx` call. Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-064), `Auctioneer` carries a third, independent population — one `reliability.CircuitBreaker` per auction winner name, threshold 3/cooldown 30s. Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-065), production's `NewPersistentAuctioneer` routes that population through a process-wide `winnerBreakerStore` that also persists to `circuit_breakers.json`, namespaced under an `"a2a.auction.winner."` key prefix so it coexists with the scheduler's per-agent entries in the same file without a shared Go type; `NewAuctioneer` (ad hoc/test callers) keeps the original in-memory-only, per-instance map. Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-074), a fourth, package-level breaker (`embeddingBreaker`, `internal/knowledge/embeddings.go`) guards `EmbeddingClient.GetEmbedding`'s Ollama HTTP call — threshold 3/cooldown 60s, shared across every `EmbeddingClient` instance rather than per-instance — so a dead embeddings backend is timed out (a paired 2s `http.Client` timeout) and then skipped entirely instead of being re-dialed and re-timed-out on every subsequent `BuildIndex`/`discoverWithEmbeddings` call. |
| **Claude Backoff** | Durable rate-limit window for the GOAP fusion loop: a rate-limited Claude outcome persists an RFC3339 `goap_fusion_claude_backoff_until` deadline (agent-scope blackboard, ChainState fallback); while active, both Claude consumers skip in milliseconds — the plan-resume runtime degrades to ScheduledAnalysisPath preserving its carryover, the review fallback returns rate-limited without invoking Claude. Half-open: an elapsed or malformed deadline reads as inactive. `BT_GOAP_CLAUDE_BACKOFF` (default 6h) on the implementation path, fixed 1h on the review path. ADR-016. |
| **CMA-ES** | Covariance Matrix Adaptation Evolution Strategy (`internal/evolution/cmaes.go`): continuous optimization of node-level numeric parameters (timeouts, retry counts, thresholds) rather than tree topology. `TuneTreeParameters` is the production seam — Extract→Optimize→Apply on clones, never mutating the input; reached via `bt_evolve_bottlenecks`, which routes parameterized trees to CMA-ES and parameterless ones to genetic evolution (ADR-020). |
| **Condition** | A leaf node that evaluates a boolean predicate. Used in PreGate and OutcomeSelector for branching decisions. |
| **Crisis Detector** | Proactive population-health monitor (`internal/evolution/crisis_detector.go`), the counterpart to the reactive QualityGate. `DetectPopulation` flags `diversity_collapse`/`regression_spiral`/`quality_crash`; on a hit, the shared `Population.selfHealGeneration` envelope overrides the generation's mutation rate with the emergency rate (μ_emergency = 0.50) and resets streak counters after a spiral. Reachable in production via `bt_evolve_qd` (`Evolve`), via `bt_evolve_genetic`/`bt_evolve_bottlenecks`/`bt_evolve_selection_pressure`'s live-bank `EvolveWithExperience` path (ADR-038 milestone 2), via `bt_evolve_island`'s per-island `EvolveAll` (ADR-038 milestone 5), and via `bt_evolve_multiobjective`'s `NSGAIIPopulation.Evolve` (ADR-051) (`EvolveQLearning`/`MemeticEvolve` remain outside it, and `ParetoPopulation.EvolvePareto` — ADR-038 milestone 4 — runs it but has no `bt_evolve_*` caller yet). Its `SpecialistRegistry` companion preserves the best validated `specialist:<type>` archetype for crisis resurrection; the observe→resurrect loop runs wherever `selfHealGeneration` does, plus the MAP-Elites illuminator `EvolveMAPElites` (milestones 4–5) via its own copy of the same logic — live in prod since `newProductionPopulation` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) seeds every MCP-tool population's registry via `SeedSpecialistRegistry`. Self-healing signals are exported as one read-only value via `Population.HealthSnapshot()` (`PopulationHealth`: crisis reasons, applied mutation rate, generation, resurrection count — ADR-032). ADR-031, ADR-038. |
| **Dead Letter Queue (DLQ)** | Persistent JSON file (`dead_letter_queue.json`) that stores tasks whose retries have been exhausted. Bounded at `MaxDeadLetterEntries` (1000) with oldest-first eviction. The dashboard flags an entry for retry via `Requeue` (stamps `RequeuedAt`, keeps the entry on disk) rather than destructively replaying it cross-process; an entry whose replay `Attempts` reach `MaxReplayAttempts` (5) is terminally flagged `Abandoned` and excluded from further auto-requeue. The file is multi-writer (daemon, dashboard, MCP siblings): saves are atomic (tmp+rename) and re-merge on-disk entries under a `<path>.lock` sidecar flock so sibling requeue stamps survive; a corrupt file is quarantined to `<path>.corrupt` instead of silently restarting (and re-persisting) an empty queue; and every cross-process consumer — the daemon's replay scan, `bt_dlq_replay`, `bt_dlq_list`, the dashboard DLQ panel — calls `Reload()` before consuming; `Push` also defaults an empty `ID` (agent name + nanosecond timestamp + a push counter) so callers that never set one, like the HITL-exhausted escalation path, don't collide and cross-contaminate each other's replay state under the ID-keyed merge. ADR-025, ADR-036, ADR-047. |
| **Deferred Outcome** | A graceful, expected scheduler pause (e.g. the `goap_fusion_rate_limited` Claude rate-limit carryover) recorded via `SLOMetrics.RecordDeferred` into a dedicated `DeferredCalls` counter. It is terminal — neither retried nor dead-lettered — and deliberately leaves the success/failure counters and latency totals untouched so a backoff never inflates the success rate or success-latency the gardener's validation gate reads. ADR-025. |
| **DefaultTree** | The fallback behavior tree used when no specific tree matches. Extracted from a 750-line god node into 21 paths across 7 category files. |
| **Deploy Drift** | The condition where a long-running daemon (bt-agent, bt-dashboard, bt-gardener) serves code older than repo HEAD after an autonomous landing. Diagnosed by `DriftStatus` (`internal/agent/deploy_drift.go`), which shells `git rev-parse HEAD` and compares it to the running Build Identity; an unstamped revision is never reported stale. Detection-only by default (`StartDriftWatcher`, every scheduler cycle); out-of-place rebuild+swap (`rebuild.go`, `RebuildBinaries`) is opt-in via `BT_AUTO_REBUILD_ON_DRIFT` and does not itself restart the process. Each of the three mains passes its own `RebuildTarget` list — `bt-dashboard` uses `DashboardRebuildTargets`, which (unlike `DefaultRebuildTargets`) includes bt-dashboard's own binary (ADR-045), so its watcher can actually adopt a fix rather than only rebuilding its siblings. A `RebuildBackoff` guard and `Scheduler.AnyInFlight()` throttle retry-storms and avoid swapping mid-job (ADR-045); both are now wired into production (ADR-050) — all three mains set `Backoff: agent.NewRebuildBackoff()`, and `bt-agent` additionally sets `InFlightFn: globalSched.AnyInFlight` since it is the only one of the three that owns a live `Scheduler` (`bt-dashboard`/`bt-gardener` have no equivalent guard). Program 94b0b31, ADR-044, ADR-045, ADR-050, R13. |
| **Evolution** | The process of systematically improving behavior trees through mutation, fitness evaluation, and selection. 6 algorithms available. |
| **Experience Bank** | Persistent store of successful mutation experiences (`~/.go-bt-evolve/experience/experience.json`), EvoRepair-inspired. Warm-starts `EvolveWithExperience` operator selection via tree-type retrieval; only fitness-improving mutations are recorded. Wired into `bt_evolve_genetic` and `bt_evolve_bottlenecks` (ADR-017) and shared with bt-gardener, whose v2 cycles record accepted mutations and bias candidate ordering from the same file (ADR-021). Bounded at 500 entries: quality-aware eviction (lowest quality/oldest first, `TimesReused >= 3` protected) enforced on Add and on load (ADR-018). Safe for the two concurrent writers: every full-file write path (Add, MarkReused, Persist) re-merges on-disk entries by ID and holds an exclusive flock on the `experience.json.lock` sidecar across merge→rename (ADR-022, ADR-024). Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-062), `TransferExperiences(sourceTree, _)` genuinely reads `sourceTree`'s own entries (`RetrieveByTreeType`) and `SeedDomain(sourceTree, targetTree)` copies up to 5 of them into `targetTree`'s pool, re-tagged as native — the mechanism an optional `IslandModel.Bank` field uses to make `Migrate` seed a migration target domain's experience pool from its source, though no production `bt_evolve_island` call wires `.Bank` yet. |
| **Expert Knowledge** | Curated design patterns (6) and anti-patterns (5) that guide tree evolution. Includes TreeArchetypes for each category. Since 2026-07-15 (ADR-095), `ExpertKnowledge` also carries a durable `LearnedPatterns` archive alongside this hardcoded catalog: `Observe(action, category, gain)` (nil-safe, non-positive gains discarded) records genuinely fitness-improving mutations, `Population.EvolveQLearning`/`qLearnMutate` (`internal/evolution/learning.go`) call it for every mutation via an optional `ek *ExpertKnowledge` parameter, and `Save`/`Load` persist it as JSON to `~/.go-bt-evolve/expert_archive-<tree>.json` — one file per sanitized base tree, mirroring `QTable.Save`/`Load` (ADR-041) but with no `Cap`. `bt_evolve_qlearning` warm-starts and re-persists it every call; `bt_evolve_expert` warm-starts from the same file before `RecommendMutations`/`DetectAntiPatterns` and returns the merged `learned_patterns` alongside the hardcoded catalog's recommendations, so advisory calls surface cross-run learning instead of only the static benchmark-derived rules. |
| **Fitness Score** | Multi-dimensional evaluation of a behavior tree's performance. Dimensions include correctness, completeness, conciseness, actionability. |
| **Gardener** | The evolution orchestrator (`cmd/bt-gardener`). Runs evolution cycles: evaluate → order mutations (experience-biased, ADR-021) → apply → re-evaluate → accept (recorded to the shared Experience Bank) or rollback. Each cycle's outcome is a `CycleMetrics` record (a `crisis_intervention`/`CrisisIntervened` flag pair and a `MutationBudget` int when the cycle-level crisis detector boosted the mutation budget), persisted to `~/.go-bt-gardener/gardener-metrics.json` as an aggregate document — `last_run`, `total_crisis_interventions`, and, since 2026-07-13, `total_cycles`/`active_trees`/`best_fitness`/`total_improvements` too, alongside the full history — that the dashboard's gardener panel parses (ADR-032). |
| **GOAP** | Goal-Oriented Action Planning. PlannerNode extends UtilitySelector with goal management, world state, and available actions. |
| **GOAP Fusion Loop** | The scheduled self-improvement cycle (`domain:goap_fusion_loop`, cron 0,30): research → goals → plan → implement → verify → land, wired with Phase-0 preflight, circuit gates, and state-hash producers. |
| **Grill** | Multi-turn critical NotebookLM review ("what is the framework missing?") rotating rounds 1-3 across cycles; answers feed the shared goal list. Round questions are served from the per-day query cache. |
| **HITL** | Human-in-the-loop approval gate node (HumanApprovalGate). Requests persist under `~/.go-bt-evolve/hitl/`; policy can auto-approve. Since ADR-056, an escalated request (`StatusEscalated`, set by `Store.Escalate` via `POST /api/hitl/<id>/escalate`) is resolvable: `Store.Approve`/`Store.Reject` accept it as a valid pre-state alongside `StatusPending`, and `Store.WaitForRequest` terminates on it immediately instead of polling until context cancellation. Since ADR-061, the dashboard workflow path treats that termination correctly rather than as an implicit approval: `WorkflowApprovalWait` sets `Approved = true` only for `StatusApproved`/`StatusSkipped` (a distinct `Escalated bool` covers `StatusEscalated`), `executeStep` gives an escalated approval step its own `"escalated"` outcome that halts the pipeline like a rejection, and `FindPendingByTaskID` (backing `ApproveByTaskID`/`RejectByTaskID`) matches `StatusEscalated` alongside `StatusPending` so an escalation is resolvable by task ID too. Since ADR-068, the by-task-ID REST (`handleTaskApprove`/`handleTaskReject`) and MCP (`bt_workflow_approve`) resolution paths also report which case actually happened — `hitl_resolved_from`/`resolved_from` is `"escalated"` vs `"pending"`, and a task with no matching request surfaces the underlying error as `hitl_note` instead of resolving silently — closing the 4-milestone HITL-escalation program. Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-077), a gate's `bb.Outcome = "pending_approval"` is preserved as a non-terminal outcome all the way through `engine.RunTask` (instead of collapsing to `"partial"` at the tick limit) and through the A2A responder, where `TaskStateBridge.BTToA2A` maps it to `a2a.TaskStateInputRequired` so an A2A caller sees the task as awaiting input rather than failed. |
| **Task Approval (dashboard)** | The audit trail on `dashboard.Task` (`internal/dashboard/tasks.go`) for approve/reject decisions on thinktank-derived tasks, added by mirroring the `Approval` struct `workflow_engine.go` already declared for its own `WorkflowTask`. `TaskStore.Approve`/`Reject` set `ApprovedBy`/`ApprovedAt` or `RejectedAt`/`Reason`/`IsApproved` and persist through the normal save path; `Approved()` — unchanged in that it still returns only tasks a human has explicitly approved — now sorts them by `workflow_engine.go`'s `WorkflowPriority` ordinal (critical first) then `Sprint`, so `handleSprintExecute` dispatches the most urgent approved work first. ADR-072. Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-080), `handleAnalyze` populates `dashboard.Task.Approval` from a real `Workflow.RecommendationsToTasks`-derived `WorkflowTask.Approval` rather than the zero-value default — `Workflow` itself (`NewWorkflow`/`RecommendationsToTasks`/`Prioritize`) is no longer production-callerless. Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-081), `Workflow.PendingApprovals`/`ApproveTask`/`RejectTask` also gained production callers, via `GET /api/workflow/pending` and `POST /api/workflow/approve`/`/api/workflow/reject`, and `RunFullPipeline` no longer auto-approves `Priority <= PriorityHigh` tasks — but those endpoints mutate a `currentWorkflow` copy of the tasks, separate from the `dashboard.Task`/`TaskStore.Approve`/`Reject` audit trail this entry otherwise describes; the two approval records do not stay in sync, and `ExecuteSprint`/`RunFullPipeline` remain production-callerless. Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-082), each `WorkflowTask.ID` `RecommendationsToTasks` mints carries a random per-call nonce, closing the same-wall-clock-second `dashboard.Task.ID` collision ADR-080 introduced, and `currentWorkflow`/`Workflow.Tasks` are mutex-guarded against concurrent HTTP access — the two-parallel-records divergence noted above is otherwise unchanged. Since 2026-07-14 (ADR-086), `handleWorkflowApprove`/`handleWorkflowReject` also call `TaskStore.Approve`/`Reject` on the composed `wf.ID+"-"+wt.ID` record immediately after the `Workflow`-side decision succeeds, so a decision made through `/api/workflow/approve`/`/api/workflow/reject` now reaches the same `taskStore` record `handleSprintExecute` dispatches from — reconciled in that direction; a decision made through `/api/tasks/approve`/`/api/tasks/reject` still does not update `currentWorkflow.Tasks`, though that direction never gated sprint dispatch. |
| **Impact Graph** | Change-impact analysis mapping each source file to the tests a change to it can affect, via import-based edges and directory proximity (`BuildImpactGraph`/`ImpactGraph.TestsFor`, `internal/knowledge/impact.go`). `ImpactedTests(root, source)` is the single build+query entry point; since ADR-052 it has two production consumers — the `bt-agent-cli impact <source-file>` subcommand and the `bt_impact_tests` MCP tool — neither yet wired into `make check-quick` or the pre-commit hook. Both consumers normalize their input path via `NormalizeImpactSource(root, source)` and return an explicit error for a path outside `root` rather than a silent empty test list (ADR-070/ADR-071). |
| **Knowledge Store** | Content-hash-deduplicating research memory (`~/.go-bt-evolve/research/knowledge.json`): findings, vault notes, NotebookLM answers, and implemented goals; consulted before any research is reported. |
| **Partial Landing** | Multi-task run semantics: per-task snapshot commits let a later task's failure discard only its own edits; completed verified work still lands and the failed goal carries forward. |
| **Program / Milestone** | A research-proposed multi-cycle change (title + file-scoped milestones) persisted in `programs.json`; each cycle executes the next pending milestone at [P0] queue head and marks it done on a verified apply. |
| **Quota Economy** | Per-Pacific-day NotebookLM answer cache + daily budgets (queries/research starts) enforced at the nlmRun choke point; over budget the ResearchRouter falls back to Claude review, and a doubly-unavailable stage (fallback rate-limited too) skips non-fatally to vault context. |
| **Superpowers Run** | One durable implementation run: typed state (run.json), plan, per-task RED/GREEN evidence, verification artifacts, finish report — under `docs/superpowers/runs/<id>/`. |
| **Island Model** | An evolution algorithm where sub-populations evolve in isolation with periodic migration of top individuals. Reachable in production via the deterministic `bt_evolve_island` MCP tool; its optional `domains` parameter seeds one island per registered domain tree, matching the type's stated purpose of maintaining genetic diversity across domains. Durable as of ADR-033: `Save`/`Load` persist per-domain subpopulations (genome-deduped, fitter-copy-wins merge) to `~/.go-bt-evolve/island_archive-<tree>.json` — one archive per sanitized base-tree ID (ADR-034) — which the tool warm-starts from and re-persists on each call. Evolved-fitness write-back follows seeding (ADR-035): in domains mode each seeded `domain:<name>` entry is credited with its own island's best elite; in default mode the base tree alone receives the cross-island best. Since ADR-038 milestone 5, `EvolveAll` runs each island's generation through the shared `Population.selfHealGeneration` envelope (seeding `Specialists`/`Crisis` for islands that lack them) instead of a bare `Evaluate`, so a collapsing island resurrects an extinct specialist archetype before its elites migrate; per-island `Resurrections` aggregate into `IslandStats.Resurrections`, though `bt_evolve_island`'s JSON response does not yet surface it. Bounded as of ADR-040: `Cap` (per-island individuals) and `IslandCap` (distinct island keys) are enforced on every `Load`, evicting lowest-fitness individuals and lowest-`BestFitness` adopted islands respectively; `bt_evolve_island`'s `population_cap`/`island_cap` parameters set them before `Load`, defaulting to 3x the call's own population/island counts when omitted. Since ADR-062, an optional `Bank *ExperienceBank` field makes `Migrate` call `Bank.SeedDomain(srcDomain, tgtDomain)` for every domain pair that actually migrated an individual, transferring the source domain's own experience entries into the target's pool — implemented and tested, but `bt_evolve_island` does not yet set `.Bank`. Since 2026-07-15 (ADR-096, milestone 2/4), `bt_evolve_island`'s durable-archive save is gated: the handler now captures `EvolveAll`'s per-domain best-tree map (previously discarded), picks the seeded domain with the highest structural fitness as the cross-island winner, and runs it and the base tree through `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner` immediately before `im.Save` — a winner that regresses on the tree's real `internal/benchmark` suite skips the save, reporting `benchmark_gate_rejected` rather than trusting `StructuralMultiFitness` alone. |
| **Knowledge Graph** | In-memory graph of all 41+ trees with capabilities, keywords, embeddings, and cross-tree relationships. Powers discovery and auto-creation. Its runtime-feedback fields (Fitness, RunCount, EvolvedCount, LastOutcome, LastDuration) and `uses_tool` edges can be snapshotted to / restored from an atomic JSON file via `feedback_persist.go` (`SaveFeedback`/`LoadFeedback`, debounced `FlushFeedback`) — now wired into the `internal/agent` scheduler lifecycle (`SchedulerConfig.FeedbackPath`: load on startup, throttled flush after each run, forced flush on Stop), so feedback survives restarts and the learn→evolve loop compounds. `RegisterEvolved(baseID, evolvedID, nodeCount, fitness)` (ADR-042) creates-or-updates a genetic-family evolved winner's own tree entry — inheriting the base tree's category/capabilities/keywords, folding fitness into `StructuralFitness` via the ADR-028 monotone/clamped `evolvedFitness` helper, and connecting `baseID → evolvedID` via an `evolved_from` edge so `DiscoverRelated` surfaces it. Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-078), `RegisterEvolved` returns a `bool`: the `evolved_from` edge always connects, but the `NodeCount`/`EvolvedCount`/`StructuralFitness` write-back only happens when the entry is new or the new `fitness` beats the stored `StructuralFitness`, and `persistEvolvedWinner` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) skips persisting the tree file entirely when it returns `false` — so a weaker later winner can no longer overwrite a stronger one already on disk. Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-083), a new non-mutating `EvolvedFitnessImproves(evolvedID, fitness)` peek decides whether `persistEvolvedWinner` attempts the disk write at all, and `RegisterEvolved` itself is now called only after that write reports success — so the knowledge-graph bookkeeping and the tree-store file can no longer diverge when validation fails partway through. |
| **MAP-Elites** | Multi-dimensional Archive of Phenotypic Elites. Maintains a grid of high-performing individuals across behavioral dimensions for quality diversity. The grid has durable `Save`/`Load` (fitter-copy-wins niche merge under the ADR-024 flock) bounded by an optional `Cap` that evicts lowest-fitness cells first (ADR-033). Reachable in production via the deterministic `bt_evolve_qd` MCP tool, which warm-starts from and re-persists to a per-base-tree `~/.go-bt-evolve/map_elites_archive-<tree>.json`, setting `Cap` (default `population*5` via an optional `archive_cap` parameter) before `Load` and reporting `warm_started`/`archive_load_error`/`archive_save_error` (ADR-043). |
| **MCP** | Model Context Protocol. JSON-RPC 2.0 over stdio. 3 servers (bt-agent, bt-evaluator, bt-langagent) expose 71 total tools to Hermes Agent. |
| **Memetic Evolution** | Genetic algorithm hybridized with per-individual local search refinement (`Population.MemeticEvolve` + `LocalSearcher`, `internal/evolution/local_search.go`). Three strategies: hill-climb, simulated annealing (Metropolis acceptance), tabu search (genome-hash tabu list). Reachable in production via the deterministic `bt_evolve_memetic` MCP tool, which rejects unknown strategy values instead of silently defaulting (ADR-019). |
| **MetaValidator** | A P0 structural-safety gate on candidate trees (`internal/evolution/meta_validator.go`), distinct from and consulted after fitness/quality/SLO scoring: root-type, node-name, size/depth-limit, selector-shape, retry-bound, `ExpertKnowledge` anti-pattern, archetype-fit, fitness-floor/regression, and baseline structural-regression checks combine into one explainable `MetaAccept`/`MetaWarn`/`MetaReject` decision (`ValidateMutation`). Since 2026-07-14 (ADR-088), `Gardener.evolveTreeV2` consults it as the last check before committing a candidate that already cleared `QualityGate.ValidateFor`, so a structurally broken mutation (empty selector, unbounded retry, expert antipattern) is rejected regardless of composite fitness — wired via an optional `Config.MetaValidator` (nil preserves the historical fitness-only behavior; `cmd/bt-gardener/config.go` sets production-default thresholds). |
| **Mutation** | A structural change to a behavior tree. 10 operators: add_before, add_after, wrap_retry, prune, swap_children, rename_node, change_type, insert_fallback, clone_subtree, delete_subtree. |
| **NSGA-II** | Non-dominated Sorting Genetic Algorithm II (`NSGAIIPopulation`, `internal/evolution/multi_objective.go`): fast non-dominated sorting + crowding distance over fixed `DimSuccessRate`/`DimNodeEfficiency`/`DimStability` objectives, embedding `*Population` for crowded-tournament selection, SBX crossover, and polynomial mutation. Reachable in production via the deterministic `bt_evolve_multiobjective` MCP tool, which reports best node count, per-dimension bests, and Pareto-front size (ADR-009). Since ADR-051, `NewNSGAIIPopulation` seeds a nil-safe `Specialists` registry and `Evolve` runs each generation through the shared `Population.selfHealGeneration` envelope (re-running `Evaluate` first so `FitnessVecs`/`Fronts`/`CrowdingDist` stay in sync with the self-healing step's re-sort), and `bt_evolve_multiobjective` overwrites the default empty registry with a `SeedSpecialistRegistry`-loaded one and surfaces `Population.HealthSnapshot()` under `health` — the last `bt_evolve_*` GA/multi-objective/island tool to gain crisis detection and specialist resurrection. Since ADR-091, `NSGAIIPopulation` also carries `Cap`/`Archive` fields and `Save`/`Load` methods that wrap the final non-dominated front (`Fronts[0]`) in a `ParetoFront` and delegate to its persistence, and `bt_evolve_multiobjective` warm-starts from and re-persists this durable archive (`nsga_archive-<tree>.json`) every call. Since 2026-07-15 (ADR-096), that save is gated: `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner` runs front 0's lead individual and the base tree through the tree's real `internal/benchmark` suite and skips the save — reporting `benchmark_gate_rejected` — when the winner's measured success rate regresses against the base tree, rather than trusting `StructuralMultiFitness` alone. |
| **OutcomeSelector** | The final stage of the universal BT pattern: `MarkSuccessful` → `SelfCorrect` (retry, up to 3x) → `EscalateToDeepSeek`. Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-084), `MarkSuccessful` gates on `validateOutputQuality` (§8.7) rather than always succeeding, and `EscalateToDeepSeek` performs a real LLM escalation call instead of an always-succeeding stub — so the fallthrough to `SelfCorrect` and `EscalateToDeepSeek` is now reachable in production. Since 2026-07-14 (ADR-085), trees that terminate without ever routing through this stage at all — e.g. compiled GOAP fusion trees — get an equivalent backstop: `RunTask`'s terminal `validateOutputQuality` call now flips `bb.Outcome` to Failure on low-quality output, with the same structured-output exemption plus a `bb.Sandbox` exemption for gardener structural-evaluation runs. |
| **Pareto Front** | Set of non-dominated solutions in multi-objective optimization. Tracks trees that are not strictly worse than any other across all fitness dimensions. `ParetoPopulation.EvolvePareto` (`pareto.go`) breeds candidates via `SelectPareto`-driven crossover across the front and, since ADR-038 milestone 4, wraps each generation in the shared `selfHealGeneration` self-healing envelope. Reachable in production since ADR-057 via the deterministic `bt_evolve_pareto` MCP tool, which builds the population through `newProductionPopulation` (seeding `Specialists` the same way its sibling evolve tools do, rather than via `NewParetoPopulation`), evolves over the fixed `DimSuccessRate`/`DimPathCoverage`/`DimStability`/`DimNodeEfficiency`/`DimExecutionSpeed` axes via `StructuralMultiFitness` — a distinct response shape from `bt_evolve_multiobjective`, which drives the separate `NSGAIIPopulation`'s full non-dominated sort instead. Since ADR-091, `ParetoFront` gains `Save`/`Load`/`Cap` (atomic tmp+rename under the ADR-024 flock, dominance-based merge-on-load, lowest-composite-`Fitness` eviction first) and `bt_evolve_pareto` warm-starts a durable per-base-tree archive from it, merges the evolved population in via `AddFromPopulation`, and persists back — so the reported `front_size`/`diversity_score`/`best_per_dim` (still via `.Stats()`, now on the durable archive rather than the single call's `pp.Front`) accumulate across runs the same way `bt_evolve_island`/`bt_evolve_qd` already do, plus `warm_started`/`archive_load_error`/`archive_save_error`. |
| **PlannerNode** | A behavior tree node that extends UtilitySelector with GOAP goal management. Selects actions based on world state and goal satisfaction. |
| **PreGate** | The first stage of the universal BT pattern. Validates preconditions (input valid, tools available, graph fresh) before executing the strategy. |
| **Pre-Mutation Snapshot** | An on-disk copy of a tree's state before `Gardener.evolveTreeV2`'s mutation loop runs, written by `evolution.SnapshotTree`/read back by `evolution.RestoreTree` (`internal/evolution/quality_gate.go`) to/from `gardener.Config.SnapshotDir` (`~/.go-bt-gardener/snapshots/snapshot_<name>.json`, one slot per tree, overwritten each cycle). Since 2026-07-15 (ADR-093), this durable snapshot survives a process crash mid-cycle (the in-memory `originalTree` clone used for the same cycle's ACCEPT/ROLLBACK comparison does not), and `Registry.RollbackTree` restores + durably re-persists it on demand via the `gardener_rollback` langchain tool, recovering a bad mutation without rerunning a full cycle. |
| **Q-Learning** | Reinforcement learning algorithm. State→Action mapping with epsilon-greedy exploration. Used for mutation strategy selection. Reachable in production via the deterministic `bt_evolve_qlearning` MCP tool, whose `Population.EvolveQLearning` drives per-generation mutation-category selection through `QTable.GetState`/`SelectAction`/`Update` and reports the learned greedy policy (`LearnedActions`) alongside the evolved winner (ADR-019). Durable as of ADR-041: `QTable.Save`/`Load` persist `Values` (state→action→Q-value) to `~/.go-bt-evolve/qtable_archive-<tree>.json` — one archive per sanitized base-tree ID, mirroring the island archive's per-tree scoping — which the tool warm-starts from and re-persists on each call, reporting `warm_started` and `learned_states_before`/`learned_states_after`. An optional `Cap` bounds `Values` by evicting the least-recently-updated state first (`EvictedStates` counter); `bt_evolve_qlearning` sets it via an optional `state_cap` parameter (default `population*10`) before `Load`, and reports the resulting `evicted_states` (ADR-041 milestone 4). |
| **Recover** | Synchronous counterpart to `SafeGo` (`internal/reliability/panic_handler.go`) — runs a function inline, recovering any panic into a returned `error` instead of spawning a goroutine. Used where a panic must be handled as a normal error return within the caller's existing control flow rather than logged and forgotten: `internal/llm/health.go`'s per-tick `Probe()` call (ADR-048); since 2026-07-13 (ADR-053), `internal/llm/fallback.go`'s `FallbackLLM.generate`, which wraps each fallback model's `call(model.LLM)` invocation so a panicking model becomes that model's error and the loop proceeds to the next one; since 2026-07-13 (ADR-059), `internal/agent/webhook_publisher.go`'s `WebhookPublisher.loop`, which wraps each `p.handleEvent(event)` call so a panicking event handler degrades just that one event instead of ending the loop; and since 2026-07-13 (ADR-060), `internal/security/apikey.go`'s `KeyRotationScheduler.rotate`, which wraps each `onRotate(oldHash, newKey)` callback invocation so a panicking caller-supplied callback degrades just that rotation instead of ending the loop. |
| **RetryWithBackoff** | Exponential backoff with full jitter. 3 retry classes: standard (500ms base), LLM-specific (1s base), unknown (1s base). Max 3 retries. |
| **RunTask** | Executes a behavior tree to completion. Tick loop (1000 max). Sets outcome (success/failure/partial). Validates output quality. Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-055), backstops `bb.Result` with a message naming the task and terminal outcome whenever the tree finishes without success and no node ever wrote a result, so no caller sees a blank result for a non-success run. Since 2026-07-13 (ADR-077), a `bb.Outcome` of `"pending_approval"` (a HITL gate awaiting a human) stops the tick loop immediately rather than spinning to the 1000-tick limit, and is preserved as-is instead of being stamped `"partial"` by the terminal switch. Since 2026-07-14 (ADR-085), the terminal `validateOutputQuality` call (previously run for defense-in-depth but its result discarded) now flips a `Success` outcome to `Failure` when the resolved result fails quality validation and is not a recognized structured/zero-LLM result or a `bb.Sandbox` run — closing milestone 4/4 of the OutcomeSelector program (ADR-084) for trees that never route through `outcome()`. |
| **SafeGo** | Wrapper around `go func()` that recovers panics via an optional `PanicHandler` (defaulting to log-and-continue) and prevents a single goroutine panic from crashing the process. ADR-007. Adopted across the scheduler runner, ChainAction execution; since 2026-07-12 (ADR-046) the per-candidate fan-out in `internal/a2a/auction.go`'s `CollectBids`, the per-step fan-out in `internal/dashboard/workflow_orchestrator.go`'s `executeParallel`, and the per-tree fan-out in `internal/knowledge/embeddings.go`'s `BuildIndex`; since 2026-07-12 (ADR-047) the 18 fan-out sites in `internal/engine/reactive_parallel.go`; since 2026-07-12 (ADR-048, milestones 1–3/5) `internal/llm/health.go`'s `HealthMonitor.Start()`, `internal/security/security.go`'s `RequestTimeoutMiddleware`, and `internal/reliability/scalability_probe.go`'s `ProbeMultiNodeDashboard`; since 2026-07-12 (ADR-049, milestones 4–5/5) `cmd/bt-dashboard/pipeline_handlers.go`'s pipeline-run goroutine and `cmd/bt-agent/main.go`'s `kg.BuildIndex()` startup goroutine and DLQ replay-scan ticker loop; since 2026-07-13 (ADR-053) `internal/llm/acp.go`'s ACP-subprocess-stdout scanner (via `startScanJSONLines`, reporting a panic on `scanErr`) and the two remaining bare spawns in `cmd/bt-agent/main.go` — `"scheduler-start"` and `"a2a-server-start"`; since 2026-07-13 (ADR-059, milestones 1–3/5) `internal/agent/webhook_publisher.go`'s `WebhookPublisher.Attach` loop, `internal/security/security.go`'s `RateLimiter.cleanup`, and `internal/security/session.go`'s `SessionStore.cleanupLoop` — the latter two wrap only the outer ticker loop, so a recovered cleanup-tick panic ends periodic cleanup for that process's remaining lifetime rather than resuming on the next tick; and since 2026-07-13 (ADR-060, milestones 4–5/5, closing the program) `internal/security/apikey.go`'s `KeyRotationScheduler.loop` (with a synchronous `Recover` isolating each `onRotate` call, so a panicking callback doesn't end the loop), `internal/config/watcher.go`'s `ConfigWatcher.loop`, and `internal/reliability/heartbeat.go`'s `NodeHeartbeat.cleanupLoop` (both constructors) — the latter two are single-layer like `RateLimiter.cleanup`/`SessionStore.cleanupLoop`. All three ADR-060 sites remain dormant with no `cmd/*/main.go` caller. Still unwrapped (out of scope for the ADR-046→049/053/059/060 programs): `internal/agent/deploy_drift.go`'s own `StartDriftWatcher` ticker and `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`'s sprint-dispatch goroutine — both already carry a hand-rolled `defer recover()`, just not the standardized `SafeGo`/`PanicHandler` path. |
| **Selector** | A composite node that tries children in order until one succeeds. Used for StrategyRouter (primary → fallback → last resort). |
| **Selector-Ordering Telemetry** | Durable per-Selector child success/failure statistics for the ordering optimizers (`SelectorOptimizer`, C4.5/CART `DTAnalyzer`, `internal/evolution/`). Persisted via `SaveSelectorStats`/`Save` and merged-on-load via `LoadSelectorStats`/`Load` under the ADR-024 sidecar flock + atomic tmp+rename, summing counts so telemetry accumulates across restarts and writers. Applied to a live tree by `SelectorOptimizer.ApplyLearnedOrdering` (fallback/`AlwaysSucceed` children stay last) — reachable via the `bt_evolve_selectors` MCP tool, and, since 2026-07-13 (ADR-079), on by default in `cmd/bt-gardener`: both the daemon's scheduled cycle and every MCP-triggered `gardener_run_cycle` call now run with `EvolveV2Config.SelectorOrdering=true` and `Config.SelectorStatsPath` set to `~/.go-bt-gardener/selector-stats.json` via the `wireSelectorOrdering` helper, so `evolveTreeV2` reorders each tree's Selector children from accumulated telemetry before every persist. The separate `domains.ResolveTreeID`/`SelectorStatsPathFn` build-time tree-resolver seam remains off by default. The milestone-3 trace bridge (`knowledge.RecordSelectorOutcomes`) that once fed this telemetry from executed traces was deleted 2026-07-13 as dead code — production telemetry is fed by the separate, already-live `flushSelectorTelemetry` (`internal/agent/runner.go`). ADR-029, ADR-079. |
| **Sequence** | A composite node that executes children in order until one fails. Used for PreGate and ordered execution paths. |
| **SerializableNode** | JSON-serializable intermediate representation of a behavior tree. The bridge between YAML definitions and go-bt runtime trees. |
| **Stockfish Evolution** | Adaptation of Stockfish chess engine techniques: transposition table for caching, move ordering by predicted fitness delta, alpha-beta pruning. |
| **StrategyRouter** | The second stage of the universal BT pattern. A Selector that tries execution strategies in priority order (primary → fallback). |
| **Structural Fitness** | A tree's evolved structural quality (`TreeMeta.StructuralFitness`), written back by QD/island evolution passes (`RecordRun("evolved")`) and kept separate from the runtime-success `Fitness` EMA that genuine executions maintain. Selection blends the two via `blendedSelectionFitness`, gating the structural term by `1 - coldStartConfidence(RunCount)` so real runs steadily reclaim the signal from the frozen structural score (ADR-028). |
| **Tick** | One execution pass through a behavior tree. Multi-tick decorators (Repeat) return Running (0) between ticks. Max 1000 ticks per RunTask. |
| **Transposition Table (TT)** | Cache of evaluated mutation states (`evaluator.TranspositionTable`, `internal/evaluator/stockfish.go`), keyed by `tree_hash:task_sig`. Prevents re-evaluating identical tree configurations. Key component of Stockfish evolution. `Store`/`load` evict deterministically — the entry with the lowest `InsertedAt` marker (a monotonic per-table sequence stamped on every `Store`, i.e. the least-recently-*written* entry) is removed once the table exceeds its configured `maxSize`, replacing a prior random Go-map-iteration-order eviction (ADR-094). Since 2026-07-15 (ADR-094), an optional `gardener.Config.TranspositionTablePath` lets `Gardener.evolveTreeV2` store every processed tree's evaluation into one and `RunCycleV2` persist it after every tree, alongside `MetricsTracker.Save()` — not yet set by `cmd/bt-gardener/config.go`, so this is reachable today only from tests and the pre-existing `bt-evaluator` MCP binary. |
| **Tree Store** | Persistent storage for behavior tree definitions. Loads on startup, saves on mutation. Located in `~/.go-bt-evolve/`. Compositions saved via `bt_blocks_compose` are validation-gated before they persist or replace the live tree (§8.15, ADR-024). |
| **UtilitySelector** | A Selector variant that scores children by multi-dimensional utility and picks the highest-scoring path. |
| **Vault Manager** | Checkpoint/restore system for tree evolution. Saves tree snapshots to `~/.go-bt-evolve/vault/` for rollback. |
